## [PlaceReasoner-Beta: Reasoning-Driven Macro Placement and Benchmarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21263) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PlaceReasoner-Beta reformulates VLSI macro placement as a closed-loop reasoning problem, using a verifier-guided multi-agent framework where a vision-language model (VLM) planner proposes placements from floorplan images, macro specs, and connectivity, while geometric and physical verifiers plus a post-route optimizer refine candidates. The authors also release PlaceReasoner-Bench, an open end-to-end benchmark of 8 open RTL designs at two aspect ratios (16 tasks) evaluated by routed PPA and DRC; on it, the method achieves the best timing among DRC-clean methods on all square tasks, cutting post-route TNS by 61.2% at 1:1 and 53.0% at 2:1 versus a classical baseline field. Macro placement is a fundamental and increasingly difficult stage of VLSI physical design, and this work shows that reasoning over spatial structure under real physical-design feedback can beat proxy-objective optimization, potentially improving end-to-end layout quality for chip designers. The open benchmark also addresses a reproducibility gap, giving EDA and AI-for-design researchers a common, routed-PPA-based evaluation platform. The framework never explicitly optimizes wirelength, yet it shortens routed wirelength on most designs, and it is evaluated with routed PPA and DRC rather than pre-route proxies. The benchmark fixes floorplans and I/O assignments so methods differ only in macro positions and orientations, and the reported gains are relative to a classical baseline field rather than a single prior method.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: In VLSI physical design, macro placement decides where large circuit blocks (macros) go on the die, strongly influencing timing, congestion, and routability. Traditional automated approaches optimize hand-crafted proxy objectives such as estimated wirelength in a one-shot numerical optimization, which limits their use of visual layout context, codified design expertise, and downstream feedback. Vision-language models (VLMs) can jointly interpret images and text, and verifier-guided multi-agent frameworks add a supervisory verification layer to check and refine candidate outputs. PPA (power, performance, area) and DRC (design rule check) are standard final quality metrics in chip implementation.

## [Efficient Benchmarking for Evolving Production LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21267) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A production study of an analytics LLM agent serving tens of thousands of monthly active users compared random sampling, historical caching, fixed representative subsets, and IRT-based adaptive testing across 574 historical benchmark runs split chronologically into calibration and held-out periods. Multidimensional 2PL adaptive testing achieved the best score fidelity, reaching 1.03 percentage points of MAE while executing only 200 questions (38.5% of a full run), yet the team ultimately deployed difficulty-stratified fixed subsets for their operational simplicity. Teams running LLM agents in production must re-evaluate them repeatedly as models and prompts evolve, but full benchmark reruns are costly in time and compute. This study offers empirically validated guidance that difficulty-stratified fixed subsets transfer without recalibration to five other agent families and stay stable across calibration windows as short as one day, making recurring evaluation practical for real-world engineering teams. The study is based on 574 historical runs of a production benchmark and reports that multidimensional 2PL adaptive testing delivered the best fidelity, but the authors chose difficulty-stratified fixed subsets because of operational simplicity. The deployed subsets transferred without recalibration to five other agent families and remained stable across calibration windows as short as one day, and the paper closes with practical recommendations for recurring production-agent evaluation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Item response theory (IRT) is a statistical framework originally developed for educational testing that models the probability of a correct response as a function of a test taker's latent ability and item characteristics; the two-parameter logistic (2PL) model uses item difficulty and discrimination parameters. Computerized adaptive testing applies IRT to select items dynamically, so a test can estimate ability accurately with far fewer questions than a full fixed form. Stratified sampling, by contrast, divides a population into homogeneous subgroups (strata) and samples from each, which here means building a fixed benchmark subset that preserves the difficulty distribution of the full question set.

## [GameASG-Bench Tests Autonomous Game Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21293) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced GameASG-Bench, a benchmark that makes behavioral testability part of the autonomous software generation task for game development, implemented as 47 browser-native game-generation tasks spanning 12 primary genres and both 2D and 3D interaction. The benchmark combines static L1 source-level checks with browser-executed L2 checks that use semantic observations, real input, and runtime evidence, and it was evaluated across nine agent stacks. The benchmark exposes a gap that high average check pass rates can hide: across nine agent stacks the best mean L2 check pass rate reached 93.2%, yet the best strict task success rate — requiring all L1 and applicable L2 prerequisite and core requirement checks — was only 55.3% (26/47 tasks). This suggests that current autonomous software generation systems can satisfy many individual checks without reliably delivering fully compliant applications, a distinction that matters for anyone deploying agents to build real software. The evaluation protocol declares an interface specification before generation, fixing legal starting scenarios, player-level actions, stable snapshots, rejection behavior, and invariants while leaving private implementations open. For DeepSeek-V4-Flash, full tool access and larger nominal turn budgets yielded more strict task successes, but strict task success was not monotonic in reasoning effort, and the two tested harnesses each achieved 18 strict task successes with only ten tasks succeeding under both.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous software generation (ASG) aims to turn human requirements into executable applications, but producing a working application does not by itself prove that its interacting components satisfy the specified behavioral requirements. Behavioral testability refers to how well a software artifact supports testing in a given scenario, and browser-native games are a useful testbed because they combine code generation with real-time input and runtime behavior. GameASG-Bench builds on this idea by embedding executable checks and independently verified reference implementations directly into each generation task.

## [LEGIT: A Credentialing Protocol for Trustworthy AI Agent Marketplaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21325) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced LEGIT, a credentialing protocol that binds measured quality and cost per solved task to an AI agent's configuration, task domain, evaluation budget, and supporting evidence through a signed record. The protocol also links reputation from past task outcomes to the same agent identity, and allows buyers and agents to verify credential records and inspect optional visual profiles. As agentic marketplaces emerge where AI agents autonomously complete specialized tasks for buyers, buyers currently cannot easily determine which agent will perform best on their tasks, since reported benchmark scores are hard to verify or compare across tasks, software, and budgets. LEGIT could influence how these marketplaces evolve by making agent performance claims verifiable and comparable. Evaluations reveal cost differences between agent configurations with similar observed task success, and show that comparisons depend on the evaluation budget, supporting the need to bind performance measurements to the tested configuration and resource limits. A complementary analysis quantifies the deposits and fees required for reputation manipulation under a stated Sybil attack model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic marketplaces are platforms where AI agents with varying capabilities autonomously complete specialized tasks for buyers, similar to how humans hire contractors. A credentialing protocol is a system that issues verifiable, signed attestations about an entity's identity and capabilities, analogous to professional certifications or journeyman cards. A Sybil attack occurs when one actor creates many fake identities to manipulate a reputation system, which is why the paper analyzes the economic cost of such manipulation.

## [DENSE Distills Agent Traces into Shortcut Trees for Self-Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21423) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21423) introduces DENSE (Distilling Evidence from Nested Subtask Executions), a method that organizes agent execution traces into evidence-grounded nested shortcut trees to produce reusable feedback without post-hoc outcome labels. On Terminal-Bench 2.1, DENSE achieved the highest strict pass rate among tested non-privileged feedback methods across four recipient models, improving strict pass rate by 7.12–15.64 percentage points while using 19.0–43.6% fewer recipient tokens in reruns. This work addresses a practical bottleneck in agent self-refinement: task-specific verification and expert annotation are expensive to scale, so distilling feedback directly from existing execution traces could make iterative agent improvement cheaper and more autonomous. If validated, the approach could reduce reliance on external supervision for LLM agents deployed in terminal and software-engineering environments. DENSE compresses redundant attempts, reconciles issues across levels using recovery evidence, and summarizes completed branches while expanding unresolved ones, linking reusable progress to remaining obligations. The paper also introduces REFIT, a source-paired protocol that compares feedback from shared initial trajectories under post-hoc outcome blindness, with environments and model contexts reset for fresh attempts; GPT-5.5 ablations support combining nested subtask analysis with shortcut construction and issue reconciliation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents that operate in terminals or code environments generate long execution traces containing attempts, failures, and recoveries, but turning those traces into useful feedback typically requires outcome labels or human annotation. Terminal-Bench 2.1 is a benchmark of 89 curated tasks spanning software engineering, system administration, data processing, model training, and security, used to measure agent performance on realistic terminal work. Trajectory distillation is an emerging technique that extracts reusable skills or lessons from agent traces, and DENSE extends this line by structuring evidence hierarchically rather than as flat summaries.

## [GVPO++ Improves LLM Post-Training Stability Over GRPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21432) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (arXiv:2609.21432) introduces Group Variance Policy Optimization (GVPO), a post-training method that integrates the analytical solution of KL-constrained reward maximization into its gradient weighting scheme. GVPO guarantees a unique optimal solution and enables flexible sampling distributions without requiring importance sampling, and it naturally extends to on-policy distillation (OPD). Training instability from importance sampling is a major obstacle to deploying reinforcement-learning-based post-training methods like GRPO at scale. By providing theoretical guarantees and removing the need for importance sampling, GVPO could make LLM post-training and on-policy distillation more reliable and versatile for practitioners. GVPO's gradient corresponds to the mean squared error between the central distance of implicit rewards and that of actual rewards, giving an intuitive interpretation. The paper claims it enables optimization of a broad family of extended OPD objectives, providing a principled foundation for diverse objective design, though as an arXiv preprint its impact is not yet fully validated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement-learning algorithm for fine-tuning LLMs where completions to the same prompt form a group and advantages are calculated relative to other rewards in the group. KL-constrained reward maximization is a standard RLHF objective that maximizes reward while penalizing divergence from a reference policy, and its analytical solution is central to methods like DPO. On-policy distillation (OPD) shifts sampling from a static corpus to the student's own evolving policy, letting the student correct its own mistakes in its own state space.

## [LogicTrack audits LLM reasoning steps with formal logic solvers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21492) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LogicTrack is a neuro-symbolic framework that auto-formalizes each step of a large language model's chain-of-thought into symbolic representations and verifies it with automated theorem provers. It introduces a Solver-Based Backtracking Reward (SBR) that scores step-wise logical soundness, guides backtracking tree search at inference time, and is further used to generate supervised fine-tuning data with backtracking traces. Existing chain-of-thought optimization mostly relies on outcome-based feedback, so a model can reach a correct final answer through logically flawed intermediate steps; LogicTrack targets this gap by verifying intermediate validity rather than only the final answer. Experiments across 8 reasoning benchmarks and 7 LLMs show improved verifiability of reasoning chains and higher final answer pass rates, which matters for high-stakes domains where trustworthy reasoning is required. The framework's step-wise reward quantifies logical soundness and drives backtracking tree search at inference time, and the same trajectories are converted into SFT data so fine-tuned models internalize step-wise auditing. The abstract reports gains on 8 reasoning benchmarks and 7 LLMs, but as an arXiv preprint it has not yet undergone peer review and no community discussion is available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting asks a model to write out intermediate reasoning steps before giving an answer, which often improves accuracy but does not guarantee that those steps are logically valid. Neuro-symbolic methods combine neural language models with symbolic reasoning, and automated theorem provers are programs that mechanically check whether a logical statement follows from given premises. LogicTrack applies this combination to audit reasoning trajectories, auto-formalizing each step and checking it with a prover instead of trusting the model's own text.

Researchers introduced PolyBridgeBench, an executable benchmark that evaluates multimodal large language models (MLLMs) on generating and repairing complete load-bearing bridge designs. Models receive a visual scene and structured engineering constraints, produce a node-member-material topology, and must repair designs after dynamic physics simulation exposes failures, with experiments across six MLLMs and 189 levels. This benchmark addresses a gap in evaluating whether MLLMs can produce engineering designs that actually work when executed, not just visually plausible or structurally valid ones. It could push AI-for-engineering research toward executable, simulation-grounded evaluation and expose how far current models are from reliable autonomous design. The benchmark separates deterministic validity, dynamic functional success, and post-failure recovery, and gates execution through deterministic legality checks before native dynamic physics simulation. Results show a substantial gap between deterministic validity and dynamic success, pronounced sensitivity to material budgets, and limited post-failure recovery under the primary strict-budget setting.

## [Round-Trip Study Finds Natural Language Serialization of Tree Expressions Is Lossy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21509) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21509v2) proposes a round-trip protocol in which one model generates a word problem from a procedurally generated arithmetic expression and a separate model extracts the expression back, using symbolic equivalence as an exact oracle. Testing all pairwise combinations of sixteen models reveals that swapping generator and extractor shifts accuracy by up to 60.4 points, with the best mixed pair reaching 92.9%. The findings suggest that serializing hierarchical structure into natural language is itself a primary limiting factor when LLMs reason in chain-of-thought or exchange free-text intermediates, which affects how multi-agent and tool-using systems should be designed. The result that different models on each end outperform the same model on both ends also points to complementarity that could be exploited in practice. At least 73.6% of round-trip failures originate at generation rather than extraction, and difficulty is driven by tree structure—operator count, depth, and right-branching—rather than model family. The channel is trainable: roughly 3,600 fine-tuning examples lift every open-weight model above untrained Gemini-3.1-Pro, and a disjoint-domain regime with new operators and vocabulary also raises every open-weight model, though a gap to the frontier remains.

**Background**: Chain-of-thought prompting has become the standard way for large language models to solve complex reasoning tasks, but because generation is sequential and token-based, the information a model writes down is inherently limited. This paper treats that written intermediate as a communication channel and asks how much tree-structured compositional content survives when it is serialized into natural language and then recovered. The round-trip protocol isolates generation quality from extraction quality by using separate models for each direction and an exact symbolic oracle for scoring.

## [Cal-OPD calibrates teacher self-deviation in on-policy distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21619) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21619v1) introduces Calibrated On-Policy Distillation (Cal-OPD), which estimates the teacher's self-deviation region via positive and negative privileged interventions and removes it from the observed teacher-student discrepancy. On mathematical reasoning benchmarks, Cal-OPD retains only about 52-65% of the original discrepancy signal yet consistently outperforms standard OPD and its variants across model scales. The work shows that a large portion of the teacher-student discrepancy signal used in on-policy distillation is actually noise from the teacher itself, so filtering it yields better reasoning performance with less signal. This matters for anyone training reasoning models via distillation, especially in privileged-information settings where teacher-side likelihood shifts are larger. The method uses positive and negative privileged interventions to delineate the teacher's self-deviation region, then keeps only the discrepancy component beyond that region as the optimization signal. The authors report that despite discarding roughly 35-48% of the original signal, Cal-OPD beats standard OPD and its variants across model scales on math reasoning benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation trains a smaller student model to mimic a larger teacher model, and on-policy distillation (OPD) has the student generate its own trajectories while the teacher grades each token, giving fine-grained feedback. The teacher-student discrepancy is the token-level difference between the teacher's and student's predicted distributions, which standard OPD treats as a pure measure of the capability gap. Privileged information refers to extra inputs available only during training, and in privileged OPD it can cause larger shifts in the teacher's own likelihoods, further contaminating that discrepancy.

A new arXiv paper (2609.21626) proposes Self-Meta-Evolve, a dual-loop hierarchical framework that maintains a dedicated prompt for each user and continuously refines it via an inner loop that edits structured prompts from persona-conditioned feedback and an outer loop that evolves the meta-prompt by distilling successful editing patterns. The authors also release a persona-driven information extraction benchmark of 292 simulated enterprise users built on O*NET occupational taxonomies, on which the method reaches a 74.58% success rate, beating the strongest prompt-optimization baseline by 13.56 absolute points and hitting 52.54% within just two iterations. Enterprise LLM deployments often need the same document reorganized differently for each user, yet existing prompt optimization methods optimize a single global prompt that ignores this heterogeneity. This work reframes enterprise information extraction as per-user prompt adaptation under interaction feedback, and its benchmark plus human study (prompts winning 71% of pairwise comparisons against static baselines) offer a practical path for personalizing LLM-based extraction in real workplaces. The framework separates concerns into an inner loop that edits structured prompts based on persona-conditioned feedback and an outer loop that improves the meta-prompt itself by distilling successful editing patterns, enabling fast adaptation (52.54% success after only two iterations). Evaluation combines the 292-user simulated benchmark with a double-blind human study of twenty real professionals, though the benchmark relies on simulated personas generated from O*NET taxonomies rather than real enterprise data.

**Background**: Prompt optimization methods such as GEPA and EvoPrompt typically search for a single prompt that maximizes a global objective across all inputs. Meta-prompting takes this a step further by using an LLM to generate or refine the instructions that guide another LLM. Self-Meta-Evolve combines these ideas with user personas, which are simulated profiles of job roles derived from the O*NET occupational database, so that each user gets a prompt tailored to how they need information extracted.

## [L0-MoE Accelerates Dense LLMs Up to 2.5x with Minimal Loss](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21672) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21672) proposes L0-MoE, a lightweight Mixture-of-Experts approach that uses L0-regularization to accelerate dense LLMs with nearly no performance loss. The method introduces a cluster confusion matrix for domain-aware dataset curation and applies dynamic batching for efficient training, achieving up to 2.5x speedup over dense models and outperforming existing LLM acceleration baselines. LLM inference is slow and expensive, and existing acceleration methods often degrade quality noticeably, so a technique that delivers up to 2.5x speedup with competitive performance could directly reduce serving costs for researchers and practitioners deploying dense models. It also offers a middle path between fully dense models and resource-hungry MoE architectures. The approach relies on L0-regularization, which penalizes the number of nonzero parameters, to sparsify a dense model into a lightweight MoE form, and it adds a cluster confusion matrix for domain-aware dataset curation plus dynamic batching for training efficiency. The reported 2.5x speedup is a best-case figure, and the paper's claims are based on the authors' own experiments against existing acceleration baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Dense LLMs use all of their parameters for every input token, which makes inference accurate but computationally heavy, whereas Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models route each input to only a subset of specialized expert sub-networks, cutting computation at the cost of large total parameter counts and complex training. Regularization techniques such as L0 penalize model complexity to prevent overfitting, and L0 specifically counts nonzero parameters, encouraging sparsity. L0-MoE combines these ideas by turning a dense model into a sparse, expert-routed one so it can run faster without retraining from scratch.

<a id="item-61"></a>
## [GUARD Achieves Natural Forgetting in Large Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21677) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21677v2) introduces Guided Answer-Reasoning Distillation (GUARD), a machine unlearning method that converts unsafe chain-of-thought disclosures in large reasoning models into safe-exit trajectories consisting of a coherent non-disclosing CoT followed by a stable refusal-style answer. The authors also propose the Natural Forgetting Reasoning Score (NFRS) to measure structural stability, fluency, and unsupported substitutes in forgotten outputs, and show on R-TOFU and a STAR-1-derived harmful-intent benchmark that GUARD substantially reduces unsafe and privacy disclosures across two distilled LRMs while preserving reasoning utility. Machine unlearning for large reasoning models is harder than for standard LLMs because protected facts or unsafe rationales can leak in intermediate chain-of-thought traces before the final answer, and existing objectives that merely suppress or redirect content can produce hallucinated substitutes, malformed boundaries, or repetitive outputs. GUARD's focus on a coherent post-forgetting trajectory and its new NFRS metric could give AI safety and alignment researchers a more reliable way to remove dangerous knowledge while keeping models usable. GUARD aligns a frozen LRM via guidance tokens and then distills the guided safe behavior into model parameters, rather than only suppressing target content. Evaluation covers two widely adopted distilled LRMs on R-TOFU and a STAR-1-derived harmful-intent setting, with NFRS capturing structural stability, fluency, and unsupported substitutes beyond simple leakage metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to selectively remove or reduce undesirable knowledge and behaviors from trained models, a problem that has become more prominent with large language models. Large reasoning models generate chain-of-thought traces—intermediate reasoning steps that improve complex problem solving—which creates new leakage surfaces because unsafe content can appear before the final answer. Knowledge distillation transfers capabilities from a teacher model to a student model, and GUARD uses this technique to teach a model a safe forgetting trajectory instead of merely erasing content.

## [ReACT-TTS Plans Conversational Speech from Listener Facial Reactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21683) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose ReACT-TTS, a two-stage framework that uses a one-second pre-response listener facial sequence to plan the emotion and prosody of the next utterance before speech realization. On a strict dyadic MELD protocol, temporal conditioning achieved higher mean macro-F1 and VAD concordance than text-only conditioning across ten seeds, and in a study with 20 speech researchers, 76% of contextual-appropriateness judgments preferred the temporal approach. This work shows that listener facial dynamics can serve as complementary cues for conversational response planning, potentially improving the emotional appropriateness of synthetic speech in interactive systems. It is a valuable technical contribution to affective computing and multimodal interaction, likely to interest researchers working on conversational TTS and human-agent dialogue. Ablations show that temporal modeling performs best among the visual variants and that an explicit early-to-late difference is unnecessary, while correct listener reactions outperform cyclic mismatches on average. The predicted response style is connected to a Grad-TTS backbone for end-to-end speech realization, and the source code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Conversational text-to-speech (TTS) aims to generate speech that fits dialogue context, but most systems condition only on text. MELD (Multimodal EmotionLines Dataset) is a dataset of emotional dialogues from the TV series Friends with audio, visual, and textual modalities, used here under a strict dyadic protocol. Grad-TTS is a diffusion-based TTS model that generates mel-spectrograms from noise using a score-based decoder and Monotonic Alignment Search, and it serves as the speech realization backbone in this work.

## [ECG Mirage: VLMs Fail to Use Patient-Specific ECGs in Clinical Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21755) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper identifies a failure mode called "ECG Mirage" in which vision-language models (VLMs) show strong predictive performance on emergency department tasks without actually relying on the correct patient's ECG. Across four VLMs evaluated on the MDS-ED dataset, matched ECGs provided no consistent advantage over mismatched or no-image inputs for ICU admission or clinical deterioration prediction, and the authors mitigated this by training restricted visual prompts that raised balanced accuracy to 70.6% and 67.5% respectively. This finding matters because it shows that high benchmark accuracy in multimodal clinical AI does not guarantee that models are using the right patient data, which could lead to unsafe or untrustworthy clinical decision support. It highlights a critical evaluation gap for medical AI systems that are increasingly being considered for real-world emergency department deployment. The study distinguishes two forms of the failure: "ECG neglect," where ECGs add little predictive benefit, and "ECG confusion," where matched ECGs outperform no-image inputs but not mismatched ECGs. The mitigation approach freezes the VLM backbone and trains four restricted visual prompts using supervised learning followed by conditional direct preference optimisation, widening the matched-versus-mismatched gap by roughly 16.5 and 5.5 percentage points for the two tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models are AI systems that jointly process images and text, extending text-only large language models to multimodal inputs such as photographs or diagrams. In emergency departments, clinicians combine heterogeneous data including patient history, vital signs, laboratory results, and electrocardiograms (ECGs), which record the heart's electrical activity. Multimodal learning aims to integrate these different data types for a more holistic understanding, but this paper shows that integration can be superficial when models appear to use ECG images without actually depending on the correct patient's tracing.

## [LLM-Generated Feature Pools Boost Time Series Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21801) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21801) shows that a simple statistical pipeline using LLM-generated feature pools achieves 0.529 per-series VUS-PR on the TSB-AD-U benchmark, surpassing the best neural (0.45) and statistical (0.44) entries and coming within 0.06 of the strongest pretrained foundation model. By selecting over the union of hand-crafted and LLM-generated pools, the pipeline reaches 0.588, matching the top leaderboard entry. This work demonstrates that the candidate feature pool, not the selection algorithm or aggregation method, is the primary performance driver in univariate time series anomaly detection, suggesting that LLMs can effectively automate feature engineering for this task. It challenges the trend of increasingly complex neural and foundation-model approaches by showing a simple statistical pipeline can be competitive. Ablations reveal that changing the candidate pool moves the score by 0.226, while selection strategies and aggregation grids only move it by 0.031 and 0.096 respectively. The LLM-generated pools match hand-crafted ones under matched selection and cover different domains, with their union improving performance in all twelve generator-seed pairs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Time series anomaly detection aims to identify unusual patterns in sequential data, and VUS-PR is a recently proposed evaluation metric designed to be more reliable than traditional point-wise measures. TSB-AD-U is a public benchmark for univariate time series anomaly detection with high-quality labeled data from diverse domains. The method uses a transductive robust MAD (median absolute deviation) model to score sliding windows, and prompts a multimodal LLM with in-context example windows to generate domain-specific feature pools.

## [MIST Uses Genomic Tokens to Guide Histology Attention for Survival Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21811) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MIST introduces a multimodal survival prediction model that represents genomic features as tokens and lets them query compact foundation-model-derived histology context tokens before survival prediction. Training combines discrete-time survival prediction with genomic feature masking, WSI dropout, and paired WSI-genomics contrastive alignment, and across four external cohorts (colon, renal, lung, glioblastoma) MIST improves external C-index over standard fusion baselines in the primary comparisons. This work offers a compact and effective strategy for multimodal oncology outcome prediction, addressing external cohort shift and computational complexity that often limit whole-slide image plus genomics fusion. It is likely to interest researchers in computational pathology, medical AI, and survival analysis who need robust models that generalize across institutions. MIST enriches molecular information with histology context rather than merging separately encoded modalities only at the final stage, and its code is available at https://github.com/samiyavuuz/MIST. The evaluation focuses on external C-index improvements in primary comparisons across four cancer cohorts, though the abstract does not report absolute effect sizes or detailed ablation results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Whole-slide images (WSIs) are gigapixel digitized pathology slides that contain rich tissue morphology, while genomic profiles capture molecular alterations; combining both can improve prognosis prediction. Survival analysis models estimate time-to-event outcomes, and the concordance index (C-index) is a standard metric measuring how well predicted risk scores rank patients by observed survival. Discrete-time survival prediction reformulates the problem into predicting event hazard within pre-specified time intervals, allowing binary classification methods to be applied. External cohort shift refers to performance degradation when a model trained on one patient population is tested on another with different characteristics.

## [EnterpriseVal: A Use-Case-Level Evaluation System for Enterprise GenAI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21841) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.21841) introduces EnterpriseVal, a use-case-level evaluation system for enterprise generative AI that formalizes the frozen socio-technical configuration under test — model, prompts, retrieval, tools, guardrails and human oversight — and maps metric vectors with confidence bounds to REJECT/CONDITIONAL/SCALE decisions via a two-tier threshold gate. A pilot across three workflows in a global bank reported 88% human-graded citation precision and a 1.6% hallucination rate in credit-memo drafting, and cut analyst refinement effort in procedure transformation from an estimated 27.4 to 2.9 hours per document. Most enterprise GenAI initiatives fail to show measurable business impact, and Gartner predicts roughly 40% of agentic AI projects will be cancelled by 2027 — largely because public benchmarks answer "what can the model do?" rather than "is this workflow fit, reliable, safe and worth scaling here, on our data, under our controls?" EnterpriseVal directly targets this measurement gap, offering enterprises a structured way to justify or reject deployment decisions before committing budget. The system combines a formal use-case specification, a metric catalogue spanning fidelity, utility, efficiency, reliability, assurance and oversight, a grading protocol that scales blinded expert judgement with calibrated LLM-as-judge scoring via prediction-powered inference, and a value-and-risk model in which the reviewer catch rate is a measured parameter. The paper explicitly separates established results, documented pilot evidence, proposed system components and open hypotheses, and specifies the experiments still required for full validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Public AI benchmarks such as MMLU or HELM measure general model capability, but they do not capture whether a specific deployment will work inside a particular organization's workflows, data and governance controls. Enterprise AI evaluation increasingly draws on socio-technical thinking, which treats the technology and the surrounding human, process and organizational systems as a single configuration rather than evaluating the model in isolation. Related governance practice also uses autonomy levels and consequence tiers to scale how much scrutiny a given AI system requires, since higher autonomy leaves less time between a decision and its irreversible consequences.

## [AutoRecLab turns natural-language prompts into recommender-system experiment code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21863) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AutoRecLab is a Python-based autonomous RecSys lab that converts a natural-language research idea into executable experiment code by deriving explicit requirements, building and validating a prototype, then iteratively expanding it into the full experiment. In a demonstration implementing an explicit-to-implicit feedback conversion study across six algorithms and three datasets, 8 of 9 runs succeeded at roughly $1 per run using GPT-5.4-mini. Empirical evaluation is central to RecSys research, yet translating experimental designs into working code remains manual and error-prone; an autonomous lab that reliably generates runnable experiments could substantially lower the engineering barrier for researchers. The combination of high success rate and low per-run cost suggests such automation may become practically viable for routine experimentation. The workflow combines retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) for documentation lookup, static type verification, and execution-steered tree search to iteratively repair and expand generated code. The reported results come from a demonstration paper rather than peer-reviewed validation, and the baseline comparison is limited to six algorithms and three datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) lets a large language model consult external documentation before producing output, while execution-steered tree search explores multiple candidate code paths and uses runtime feedback to guide the search. Explicit feedback refers to direct user ratings, whereas implicit feedback is inferred from behavior such as clicks or purchases; converting between the two is a common RecSys research question.

## [AutoViewMem Organizes LLM Long-Term Memory into Self-Configuring Views](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21940) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AutoViewMem is a new data-driven framework that discovers candidate semantic views from interaction traces, selects a compact complementary view set, and uses these views to guide write-time structured extraction of provenance-grounded memories. Experiments on the LoCoMo and PersonaMem benchmarks with Qwen3-8B and Qwen3-14B backbones show improved long-horizon question answering and personalization over strong memory baselines while keeping a simple inference pipeline. Long-term memory is essential for LLM agents to stay consistent and personalized across extended interactions, but mixing preferences, events, constraints, and temporal updates into one representation causes semantic interference that degrades retrieval. By moving semantic disentanglement from retrieval time to write time, AutoViewMem could make agent memory more reliable without adding routing or iterative retrieval overhead. The framework discovers views from interaction traces, selects a compact complementary set to minimize overlap, and performs offline consolidation to improve memory compactness and consistency. Because disentanglement happens at write time, standard top-K similarity search can retrieve focused evidence without explicit routing or iterative retrieval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents increasingly rely on external memory systems to recall past conversations, but most systems use fixed granularities or static schemas that struggle with heterogeneous information. Semantic interference occurs when related but distinct meanings are stored together, making top-K retrieval noisy and poorly ranked. Semantic disentanglement aims to separate high-level factors of meaning into independent subspaces, and AutoViewMem applies this idea to conversational memory organization.

## [Designer-RSI: Frozen Models Learn Design Skills via Procedural Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22086) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Designer-RSI introduces a continual adaptation framework in which a frozen frontier model operates professional design software through over 230 tools while an external procedural memory of natural-language skills accumulates and refines reusable design procedures. Across five rounds on 1,406 real user briefs and 1,869 automatically graded trajectories, the skill bank grew from 76 to 139 skills and raised GenEval2 execution success on Claude-Sonnet-4 from 72.7% to 99.3%, with no weight updates and no human labels. This work shows that agents can continually improve on long-horizon, hard-to-verify professional tasks such as graphic design without retraining the underlying model, which matters for any domain where outcomes lack a reliable programmatic oracle. It also suggests a practical path to adapting deployed frontier models to real user traffic, potentially reducing the need for costly fine-tuning or human labeling pipelines. The memory widens by acquiring procedures for recurring uncovered subtasks and deepens by revising existing procedures against their own successful and failed executions, while a matched replay gate admits only changes that repair failures without regressing observed successes. On 200 held-out briefs, widening or deepening alone reached 49.4% and 48.6% win rates over the no-skill agent, while their combination reached 58.5% (p = 0.025), and the framework achieved 61.8% and 67.6% win rates across four specialized design benchmarks on Claude-Sonnet-4 and Claude-Opus-4.6.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Procedural memory in AI agents refers to the 'how-to' knowledge that lets agents learn and execute skills, tasks, and action sequences, often stored in system prompts, tool definitions, or routing logic rather than in model weights. Frontier models deployed in production are typically frozen, meaning they have no built-in mechanism for internalizing what real-world use teaches them. Designer-RSI combines these ideas by keeping the model frozen and externalizing learned design procedures into a revisable memory bank.

## [dSTAR: Straggler Tolerant and Byzantine Resilient Distributed SGD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.07151) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose dSTAR, a lightweight distributed stochastic gradient descent (SGD) method that selectively aggregates gradients from the first k responding workers and filters them using an ensemble median to tolerate stragglers and Byzantine attacks. The paper theoretically proves dSTAR is (α, f)-Byzantine resilient with linear convergence, and empirically shows it maintains high accuracy while other Byzantine-resilient methods drop 40-50% under attack. Distributed training is increasingly common for large models, but slow workers (stragglers) and malicious nodes (Byzantine attacks) can severely degrade performance or corrupt models. dSTAR offers a combined defense with theoretical guarantees, potentially making large-scale distributed training more robust and reliable for practitioners. dSTAR collects updates from the first k workers to respond, then filters them based on deviations calculated using an ensemble median, which mitigates straggler impact and fortifies against Byzantine adversaries. The method achieves linear convergence and (α, f)-Byzantine resilience, and empirical evaluations show it consistently maintains high accuracy across various scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Distributed SGD splits model training across multiple workers that compute gradients and send them to a central server for aggregation. The straggler effect occurs when some workers are much slower, delaying the whole process, while Byzantine attacks involve workers sending arbitrary or malicious gradients to corrupt the model. Byzantine-resilient aggregation methods like Krum or median-based rules aim to tolerate a bounded number of such faulty workers, but often at the cost of accuracy or convergence speed.

## [Deep RL Controls Deformable Mirror for Post-Coronagraphic Wavefront Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20880) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.20880) presents a fully data-driven deep reinforcement learning method for post-coronagraphic aberration correction in a simulated high-contrast imaging testbed. The agent controls a deformable mirror using focal-plane images plus physics-informed wavefront sensing information, and successfully creates dark holes while approaching the performance of conventional wavefront control methods. Direct imaging of exoplanets requires suppressing starlight by many orders of magnitude, and this work shows that reinforcement learning can learn adaptive correction strategies directly from system interaction, potentially offering a more flexible alternative to traditional focal-plane wavefront control. If validated on real hardware, such data-driven controllers could simplify the demanding subnanometric wavefront control needed for future exoplanet imaging missions. The study is validated only on simplified simulations of a high-contrast imaging testbed, and the authors evaluate different observation representations and control strategies. The agent relies on focal-plane measurements and physics-informed wavefront sensing derived from those images, rather than explicit Jacobian computation.

**Background**: A coronagraph blocks starlight so that faint exoplanets can be seen, but optical aberrations cause residual starlight to leak through as speckles that obscure the planet. Wavefront control uses a deformable mirror to correct these aberrations with subnanometric precision, and focal-plane wavefront sensing infers aberrations directly from intensity images at the science focal plane. Deep reinforcement learning trains an agent through trial-and-error interaction to learn control policies, which this paper applies to that post-coronagraphic correction problem.

## [BI-Agent and BI-Bench: Automating End-to-End Business Intelligence with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20886) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.20886) introduces BI-Bench, described as the first benchmark for systematically evaluating LLMs on end-to-end business intelligence tasks, built from real-world BI projects and manually extracted (question, ground-truth answer) pairs from user dashboards. The authors also propose BI-Agent, a tool-augmented agent that decomposes BI workflows into search, join, and transform subtasks, plus a post-training framework using SFT and RL that yields accuracy gains of up to 40 percentage points over vanilla LLMs and up to 30 points after post-training. Business intelligence is a core enterprise function used in tools like Power BI and Tableau, yet the tedious data preparation steps before answering questions remain a major bottleneck. By showing that even frontier LLMs score below 50% on end-to-end BI and that tool-augmented agents plus domain-specific post-training can close much of that gap, this work points toward automating a large, high-value enterprise workflow and gives researchers a shared benchmark to build on. BI-Bench is constructed by harvesting real-world BI projects from public sources and manually extracting question-answer pairs from actual user dashboards, and the paper reports that frontier LLMs achieve less than 50% accuracy on it. BI-Agent orchestrates specialized data management methods across BI stages, and the reported gains (up to 40 points with vanilla LLMs, up to 30 points after post-training) are described as highly statistically significant.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional BI workflows require users to first identify relevant tables, perform data transformations, and build join relationships before they can answer business questions, which is complex and time-consuming. Large language models have shown strong data-handling abilities, prompting interest in whether they can handle these preparation steps automatically. Benchmarks are standard tools for measuring model capability on a task, and BI-Bench is positioned as the first to cover the full end-to-end BI pipeline rather than isolated sub-skills.

<a id="item-73"></a>
## [SpaceDiffusion Brings On-Orbit Generative AI to Satellite Relaying](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20899) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose SpaceDiffusion, an over-the-orbit diffusion framework for satellite-assisted image transmission that introduces a generate-and-forward (GF) relaying paradigm. The core contribution is a channel-distortion-aware diffusion theory that injects a channel-distortion correction term into the standard DDIM update, letting a single pretrained diffusion model adapt to varying packet-loss and compression distortions without retraining. Satellite uplinks are a major bottleneck in 6G networks because ground devices have limited transmit power and small antennas, causing low rates and high packet errors. By reconstructing corrupted data on orbit before forwarding, SpaceDiffusion could cut end-to-end latency and save roughly 15 dB of uplink transmit power, pointing toward generative-AI-native 6G satellite infrastructure. The framework formulates recovery of compressed and lost image tokens as an inverse problem, analytically characterizes progressive token-reconstruction error, and derives a diffusion-step activation threshold predicting when GF beats conventional decode-and-forward (DF) relaying. It also includes an energy-aware early-exit policy for efficient on-orbit deployment, though results are simulation-based and no community discussion is available yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative AI models that learn to denoise data step by step, and DDIM is a deterministic, faster sampling variant widely used for image generation and solving inverse problems such as medical image reconstruction. In satellite communications, decode-and-forward (DF) relaying decodes and retransmits data, but retransmissions add latency and consume power when packets are corrupted. SpaceDiffusion instead uses on-orbit generative AI to reconstruct corrupted images before forwarding, an approach the authors call generate-and-forward.

## [Bio-MF: One-Step EEG-to-fNIRS Generation for Real-Time Hybrid BCIs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20904) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Bio-MF introduces a latent-free one-step MeanFlow framework that generates fNIRS signals directly from EEG input, completing inference in a single network evaluation. On an RTX PRO 6000 GPU it produces one fNIRS trial in 7.0 ms, an 857x speedup over the 1000-step SCDM baseline, while improving motor-imagery decoding accuracy by up to 4.15 percentage points over EEG-only systems. Real-time hybrid BCIs that fuse EEG and fNIRS have been hampered by the slow, multi-step generation of synthetic fNIRS, so a 7 ms one-step generator could make hybrid decoding practical in live scenarios. This matters for clinical rehabilitation and assistive BCI systems where paired EEG-fNIRS acquisition is unavailable or inconvenient. Bio-MF performs direct signal-space x-prediction, converts it into MeanFlow velocity supervision, and integrates Spatial-Temporal Interactive 4D Encoding, cross-modal classifier-free guidance, and noise-level-gated FFT regularization to preserve task-relevant hemodynamic structure across heterogeneous sensor layouts. Gains of 3.37/4.15 percentage points (HbR/HbO) on Dataset 1 and 2.98/2.50 points on Dataset 2 under an unseen 64-channel montage show generalization, though evaluation is limited to two datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Motor-imagery brain-computer interfaces let users control devices by imagining movements, and EEG provides fast temporal but poor spatial information while fNIRS offers complementary hemodynamic signals with better spatial resolution. Hybrid EEG-fNIRS systems can outperform EEG-only setups, but acquiring both modalities simultaneously is often impractical, motivating cross-modal generation. MeanFlow is a recent one-step generative modeling framework that models average velocity instead of the instantaneous velocity used in Flow Matching, enabling single-evaluation generation.

## [Physically Based Rendering Performed in Diffusion Latent Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21054) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21054) introduces physically based rendering (PBR) directly inside the latent feature space learned by the variational autoencoders (VAEs) of image diffusion models. The authors modify the rendering equation and pair it with a differentiable renderer to produce latent maps that require only minimal refinement, training on a single rendered image and generalizing to changes in geometry, lighting, and camera view. This bridges classical graphics light-transport simulation with latent diffusion models, offering a physically grounded way to control generative image synthesis. If it holds up, it could give artists and researchers precise, physically consistent control over lighting, materials, and viewpoints in diffusion-generated content, an area where current models remain notoriously hard to steer. The core idea is that light transport phenomena correlate with the distribution of latent values, so the rendering equation can be adapted to operate in VAE feature space rather than pixel space. The method trains on just a single rendered image and then generalizes to scene geometry, lighting, and camera changes, though the abstract is truncated and full quantitative evaluations are not yet visible.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Physically based rendering (PBR) is a computer graphics approach that simulates how light interacts with surfaces and volumes using optics-based models, often via ray tracing or path tracing, to achieve photorealism. Latent diffusion models instead perform the diffusion process in the compressed latent space of a pretrained autoencoder (typically a VAE), which makes high-resolution image generation computationally feasible. Variational autoencoders map images to a probabilistic latent distribution and back, and their learned feature space is what this paper proposes to render into directly.

## [COPES Dataset and Three-Axis Framework Align LLMs with Lived Experience in Mental Health Peer Support](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21075) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.21075) introduces the COmmunity-centered Peer Engaged Support (COPES) dataset and a three-axis evaluation framework for assessing how well LLMs align with community perspectives when responding to mental health support-seeking queries. The authors show that post-training (SFT and DPO) on COPES improves Strategy Alignment by over 50% for general-purpose models and improves alignment in Emotion & Tone, but these gains are heterogeneous across subreddits and requested coping strategies. As professional mental healthcare access remains limited, people increasingly turn to online peer-support communities like Reddit, and this work suggests LLMs could help fill the gap of unanswered queries if aligned with lived experience rather than only clinical benchmarks. It also highlights that community-driven fine-tuning can introduce new biases, such as favoring problem-focused advice over emotion-focused support, which matters for anyone deploying AI in mental health contexts. The study compares zero-shot models with post-trained SFT and DPO variants, finding that post-training induces distributional shifts that heavily favor problem-focused recommendations while suppressing emotion-focused strategies. Improvements in alignment vary significantly across subreddits and specific mental health needs, meaning a single fine-tuned model does not serve all sub-communities equally well.

**Background**: Large language models are typically aligned to human intent through supervised fine-tuning (SFT), which trains on labeled example outputs, and Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), which optimizes preferences without a separate reward model. Zero-shot evaluation tests a model on a task without any task-specific examples, relying only on its general knowledge and prompt instructions. This paper applies these techniques to mental health peer support, where community norms and lived experience differ from clinical guidelines.

## [Task Vector Composition Dataset Aligns LLMs to Ethical Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21094) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a 12,000-instance multilingual dataset of two-option moral dilemmas covering three pairwise value conflicts (Honesty vs. Justice, Justice vs. Autonomy, Autonomy vs. Honesty) with translations into Hindi, Arabic, Spanish, and Chinese. It proposes a task vector transfer experiment that orthogonalizes value-preference directions against the general instruction-following vector, successfully isolating specific value preferences and enabling task arithmetic to flip a model's stance. This work addresses the critical challenge of aligning LLMs with human ethical values across languages, revealing hidden biases in state-of-the-art models like GPT-5-mini and Llama-3.2. The proposed task vector composition method offers a lightweight, inference-cost-free way to steer model behavior, which could impact AI safety, cross-lingual fairness, and deployment of ethical AI systems. GPT-5-mini consistently favors Honesty over Autonomy across all five languages when no policy is given, while Llama-3.2-1/3B models show strong first-option bias that is removed by both plain fine-tuning and Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), boosting accuracy above 98%. The task vector orthogonalization isolates value-preference directions from general instruction-following, enabling task arithmetic to produce a model with the opposite stance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Task vectors capture the difference between a pre-trained model's weights and those after fine-tuning on a specific task, and composing them enables multi-task learning without extra inference cost. Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a 2023 alignment technique that directly optimizes a language model on human preference pairs, bypassing reward modeling and reinforcement learning. LLM alignment aims to ensure models behave according to human values, but cross-lingual consistency and hidden biases remain open problems.

## [New Framework Measures Human-AI Collaboration by Quality and Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21117) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21117) proposes a productivity-oriented framework that evaluates human-AI collaboration as outcome quality relative to interaction cost, rather than by task completion time or output quality alone. Across two datasets spanning four tasks, the authors find that sessions with identical quality ratings can differ by up to 70 times in interaction cost. The framework makes interactional cost visible, challenging the common assumption that task success or user satisfaction ratings are sufficient proxies for productive collaboration. This could reshape how HCI researchers and AI system designers evaluate and optimize human-AI workflows. The study shows quality-cost relationships vary by task—some tasks reward extended interaction while others favor fast convergence—and that subjective user ratings are not reliable substitutes for productivity. Productive sessions are characterized by agents probing earlier and users spending less effort repairing the interaction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: AI productivity is typically measured by task completion time, economic value, or improvements in outcome quality, but these measures treat collaboration as a black box, capturing what output was produced without accounting for the interaction cost required to produce it. Drawing on economics literature, this paper reframes evaluation around the ratio of outcome quality to interaction cost, distinguishing 'productive success' from merely 'costly success'.

A new arXiv paper (2609.21133) introduces a Multilayered Evaluation Framework for Text-to-SQL systems that generate AI-augmented queries, decoupling deterministic database logic from flexible AI semantics. Tested on Google BigQuery and the academic ThalamusDB system, the framework achieves up to 97.2% overall accuracy, while traditional Execution Accuracy detects as few as 25% of correct translations and a state-of-the-art LLM autorater falsely rejects 32% of accurate queries. As data analytics platforms increasingly embed AI operators into SQL, existing deterministic evaluation metrics systematically fail, so this framework offers a reliable benchmarking standard that could shape how future AI-powered SQL generators are validated across industry and academia. The framework validates standard relational logic and AI operations separately rather than judging both simultaneously, which is the key reason it outperforms LLM-based autoraters; the paper formalizes unique evaluation failure modes and demonstrates results on both industrial (BigQuery) and academic (ThalamusDB) systems.

## [EnSol: Environment-Aware GNN Predicts Molecular Solubility with Uncertainty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21151) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced EnSol, an environment-aware probabilistic graph neural network that represents solute and solvent as separate molecular graphs and combines them via cross-attention, while injecting temperature through feature-wise modulation and predicting full solubility distributions with a mixture density network. On the SolProp and Leeds benchmarks it achieved Spearman correlations of 0.876 and 0.601, and experimental validation on diverse solute-solvent pairs yielded a Spearman correlation of 0.715. Solubility prediction is central to drug discovery, formulation, and materials design, but experimental measurement across solutes, solvents, and temperatures is costly and sparse. By removing the fixed-solvent assumption and modeling uncertainty, EnSol could make computational solvent selection and reaction feasibility screening more reliable across diverse chemical systems. EnSol learns separate graph representations for solute and solvent, uses cross-attention for solute-solvent interactions, and applies feature-wise modulation to encode continuous temperature effects. Its mixture density network outputs full solubility distributions rather than point estimates, capturing both temperature-dependent behavior and experimental uncertainty; results are reported on the independent SolProp and Leeds benchmarks plus wet-lab validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Molecular solubility describes how well a compound dissolves in a given solvent, and it influences reaction feasibility, formulation performance, separation efficiency, and solvent selection. Graph neural networks (GNNs) are deep learning models that operate directly on molecular graphs, where atoms are nodes and bonds are edges, and they have become a standard tool for molecular property prediction. Cross-attention lets a model exchange information between two separate representations, such as a solute graph and a solvent graph, while mixture density networks output a mixture of probability distributions instead of a single value.

## [VNT-PA Uses Camera Poses as Positional Encoding for Visual Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21212) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose VNT-PA (Visual Navigation Transformer with Pose Attention), a transformer planner whose context is a set of depth keyframes indexed by camera pose rather than temporal order. Trained to imitate a shortest-path planner on the ground-truth scene mesh, it reaches 93.3% success and 90.4% SPL on point-goal navigation in HM3D validation scenes, outperforming temporal-sequence and pose-as-input baselines. This shows that pose-stamped experience can directly serve as the environment representation for a learned planner, potentially removing the need to build explicit maps or topological graphs. It could benefit robotics and embodied AI by enabling reuse of experience across trajectories and more graceful degradation under localization noise. Because attention depends on pose differences rather than temporal order, frames from different trajectories can be fused at test time, and the planner degrades more gracefully under localization noise than a conventional map-based baseline. The approach also improves training efficiency compared to temporal-sequence encoding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Point-goal navigation is a foundational embodied AI task where an agent must navigate from a start location to a specified goal point using only local sensor observations and a relative goal coordinate. Transformers typically use positional encoding to inject sequence order into attention, but VNT-PA instead uses camera poses as positional encodings so attention is driven by spatial relationships. HM3D is a large-scale dataset of 1,000 building-scale 3D reconstructions used to benchmark such navigation policies.

## [Coda Reallocates Flow-Matching Budget to Endpoint Correction for VLA Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21216) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Coda, a method that reallocates part of the flow-matching integration budget in vision-language-action (VLA) policies to a single learned endpoint correction. On 50 RoboTwin Easy tasks, five-step Coda improves success from 71.64% to 74.68% over a matched five-step baseline while reducing forward latency by 30.2% compared to the default ten-step policy; a two-step configuration reaches 71.88% success with a 2.12× speedup, and the same design raises frozen official SmolVLA two-step success from 60.8% to 69.4%. This work shows that adding more integration steps in flow-matching VLA policies does not necessarily improve closed-loop success, and that a lightweight learned corrector can improve both success rate and latency. It offers a practical path to more efficient robot manipulation policies, benefiting robotics researchers and practitioners deploying VLA models under compute or latency constraints. Coda keeps the underlying policy frozen: a few-step noise-to-action trajectory is first generated, then a lightweight Transformer predicts a demonstration-supervised residual using the candidate action, source noise, and a shared observation-prefix cache, with only the corrector trained. An independent 13-task control shows a 5.69-percentage-point gain at nearly equal latency, supporting correction as an effective alternative to additional integration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Flow matching is a generative modeling approach that learns to transform noise into data by regressing vector fields along fixed probability paths, and it has become popular for training continuous normalizing flows and diffusion-like models. Vision-language-action (VLA) policies use multimodal inputs such as images, proprioceptive states, and language instructions to control robot manipulation, and flow-matching-based VLA policies generate action chunks by repeatedly evaluating an action expert. RoboTwin is a scalable benchmark for bimanual robotic manipulation with a 50-task suite built on the RoboTwin Object Dataset.

Researchers Zhiyuan Gao and colleagues released KnowDemo, a framework that extracts structured manipulation knowledge from human videos using a vision-language model (VLM) and uses it to generate diverse, valid robot demonstrations for a target workspace. The system resolves object and action descriptions against target-scene entities and geometry to guide candidate generation and screening before motion planning and simulation, and the authors fine-tuned the pretrained π0.5 model on the generated simulation data to achieve sim-to-real transfer across three tasks. The VLM-based knowledge extraction and reasoning module associates object and action descriptions with inferred task conditions, demonstration references, and permissible execution variations, separating task requirements from demonstration-specific choices. The resulting demonstrations exhibit alternative contact strategies and valid subtask orders with structured execution labels, and task-guided grasp sampling improved candidate planning success; however, the work is an arXiv preprint with no peer review or community discussion yet.

## [VLA-Scope Predicts Robot Failures Under Distribution Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21246) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced VLA-Scope, a two-stage framework that first detects out-of-distribution (OOD) inputs and classifies their shift categories, then predicts execution failure by combining the predicted shift category, action-prefix features, and execution progress features via a shared logistic regression model. Evaluated with OpenVLA on ten LIBERO-Spatial tasks using leave-one-group-out cross-validation, OOD detection reached a ROC-AUC of 0.9454, shift classification achieved 91% accuracy, and the failure predictor reached a ROC-AUC of 0.8497 after 60 executed actions, outperforming ActProbe and SAFE-MLP baselines. Reliability under distribution shift is a critical bottleneck for deploying vision-language-action models in real-world robotics, and this work shows that detecting OOD inputs alone is insufficient because models can still succeed or fail unpredictably under such conditions. By providing a practical failure-risk signal that updates as execution proceeds, VLA-Scope could enable safer robot control policies, such as triggering human intervention or fallback behaviors before a task fails. The failure predictor was evaluated independently of the OOD gate on all 1,400 OOD rollouts, and adding execution progress features improved ROC-AUC from 0.7906 to 0.8497 after 60 actions. The approach uses pooled image and language representations for OOD detection and a single logistic regression model shared across shift categories, evaluated with leave-one-group-out cross-validation on LIBERO-Spatial tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that take visual observations and natural-language instructions as input and directly output low-level robotic actions, pioneered by Google DeepMind's RT-2 and exemplified by the open-source 7-billion-parameter OpenVLA. Distribution shift occurs when a deployed robot encounters visual or linguistic conditions that differ from its training data, which can degrade reliability. LIBERO-Spatial is a benchmark suite within the LIBERO lifelong robot learning benchmark that evaluates spatial-relation manipulation tasks, and ROC-AUC is a standard metric for measuring how well a classifier separates positive from negative examples.

## [EvoPilot: Human-Gated Agents for Online Model Development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21257) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces EvoPilot, a human-gated agentic method for long-horizon online autoresearch, validated in a 37-day campaign on the retrieval system behind Video Deep Dive (VDD). The campaign covered seven directions over an hourly refreshed index of hundreds of millions of videos, and after repairing a pre-existing evaluation defect it measured a 3.20 percentage point offline improvement plus a 0.66% relative online gain in the VDD slice of Good Search Result Rate for Retention (GSRR). It addresses a core gap in agentic model development: a completed autoresearch run can still yield an invalid conclusion when code changes are no-ops, data windows leak, evaluator semantics drift, or the two arms traverse different serving funnels. The human-gated verification and durable records offer a practical blueprint for teams deploying LLM agents in production online experimentation, where invalid comparisons can silently mislead product decisions. Role-specific agents execute each round through a versioned domain skill and typed adapter, while deterministic checks enforce recorded lessons and durable records preserve both experiments and failures. A primitive autoresearch attempt had wrongly attributed a 22 percentage point offline hit-rate decline to an interaction head; EvoPilot traced it to a pre-existing evaluation defect producing output depths of 3,000 and 600, and post-study replay and mutation tests rejected invalid comparisons while admitting valid ones, with durable state recovering an interrupted round and artifact reuse saving roughly five GPU-hours.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Autoresearch refers to LLM agents that autonomously propose, implement, and evaluate model changes, typically in minutes-scale loops on self-contained programs. Online autoresearch is harder because it spans asynchronous systems, hours-long variant runs, and weeks-long campaigns that can affect a live product, so validity of the comparison becomes the central problem. Video retrieval systems, such as the one powering Video Deep Dive, surface follow-on videos after a user opens a seed video and rely on large, frequently refreshed indexes.

## [Formal Protocol for Revoking Authorization of Long-Running AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21284) ⭐️ 7.0/10

An arXiv preprint (2609.21284v1) introduces root-scoped authorization quiescence, a formal protocol for revoking authorization of long-running AI agents that persist through delegated tasks, queues, callbacks, reservations, and provider-side operations. The work defines a per-sink certificate that accounts for every cut-relevant acceptance under a retired root-epoch atom before its local fence, while permitting exact rebind to a current, independently sufficient support. Existing mechanisms such as cancellation, process exit, and credential revocation neither close every pre-cut carrier nor distinguish independently authorized shared work, leaving a real gap in agent lifecycle management. A formal treatment of revocation could influence future secure agent frameworks and is relevant to both AI safety and distributed systems. The protocol linearizes a root cut, fences old-root expansion and protected sinks, represents alternative and conjunctive authority as antichains of minimal sufficient root sets, and composes provider-frontier certificates into a cutset over registered old-root paths. A provider-free late-effect test suite matches 17/17 registered outcomes, and a separately implemented checker verifies 17/17 traces while rejecting 44/44 consistently rehashed semantic regressions; the certificate establishes root-relative authorization quiescence within its bound manifest and configuration, not global idleness, rollback, or business completion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Long-running AI agents can outlive the processes that launched them because they hold credentials, spawn delegated tasks, enqueue durable jobs, register callbacks, make reservations, and trigger provider-side operations. Traditional revocation assumes a single process or credential boundary, so it cannot guarantee that every downstream effect authorized under an old root has actually stopped. This paper addresses that gap by defining a formal notion of quiescence relative to a retired root epoch, using concepts from distributed systems such as cuts, fences, and antichains.

## [Deep-BQRL Brings Buffered Quantile Objectives to Deep RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21327) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Deep-BQRL, a model-free distributional reinforcement learning framework that extends buffered quantile objectives to neural function approximation. It learns conditional return quantiles directly from sampled transitions, builds buffered action scores from the learned quantile function, and uses ensemble disagreement for exploration, evaluated on an asset-selling optimal-stopping problem and slippery FrozenLake. Prior buffered-quantile methods were model-based and limited to small tabular problems, so making them model-free and scalable could enable risk-sensitive decision-making in realistic domains such as finance and robotics. The work also provides an interpretable way to tune behavior by choosing a target quantile. Deep-BQRL learns quantiles from sampled transitions and uses an augmented input representation so the policy can respond to trajectory information without explicitly reproducing the quantile-state recursion required by exact planning. In asset selling it achieves smaller mean cumulative point-quantile policy gaps than PPO and TRPO at the reported target levels, though the model-based UCB-BQRL retains the smallest gaps.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Distributional reinforcement learning models the full distribution of returns rather than just the expected return, which allows risk-sensitive objectives. Quantile-based RL optimizes a prescribed quantile of the cumulative-return distribution, but point quantiles can change abruptly under small perturbations, so lower-buffered quantiles average neighboring quantiles just below the target level to create a smoother surrogate. Earlier buffered-quantile methods remained model-based and relied on explicit return-law planning, restricting them to small tabular problems.
