## [Co-Evolving Zero-Day Jamming: Adaptive Attack Synthesis and Graph Attention-Based Online Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21334) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21334) introduces a two-pronged framework for zero-day jamming: an online detector that combines a graph attention network (GAT) for temporal-spectral representation learning with Dirichlet process (DP)-means clustering to jointly classify known and discover emerging jamming strategies, plus an inference-driven reinforcement learning (RL) jammer that treats the target receiver as a black box and optimizes the trade-off between attack impact and stealth. Simulations show the RL jammer achieves 33% higher attack efficacy and 67% higher stealth than benchmarks, while the proposed detector achieves 20% higher detection accuracy against that jammer than baseline detectors. Zero-day jamming — previously unseen interference strategies — is hard to detect because detectors cannot recognize patterns they have never been trained on, and existing adversarial benchmarks unrealistically assume the attacker knows the target receiver. By pairing an adaptive black-box RL jammer with a detector that can discover new attack clusters online, this work provides a more realistic evaluation loop for wireless security and adversarial machine learning, which matters for 5G/6G resilience and anti-jamming research. The detector uses a GAT to capture the global temporal-spectral structure of jamming signals and DP-means clustering to automatically determine the number of attack clusters without pre-specifying it, while the RL jammer infers the detector state via hypothesis testing rather than assuming prior knowledge of the receiver. The reported gains (33% attack efficacy, 67% stealth, 20% detection accuracy) come from simulations, so real-world validation and computational overhead of online clustering remain open questions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Jamming attacks deliberately transmit interference to disrupt wireless communication, and zero-day jamming refers to novel strategies not seen during detector training. Graph attention networks (GATs) are graph neural networks that use attention to weight neighboring nodes when learning representations, making them suited to modeling structured relationships such as time-frequency signal patterns. Dirichlet process (DP) clustering is a nonparametric Bayesian method that can automatically decide the number of clusters from data, unlike K-means which requires specifying it in advance. Reinforcement learning (RL) trains an agent to optimize a reward through interaction, here used to synthesize an adaptive jammer that balances attack impact against stealth.

## [CESBench: First LLM Benchmark for IoT Cryptographic Engineering Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21344) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced CESBench, a benchmark of 380 expert-written items covering six sub-domains of cryptographic engineering security for IoT devices: side-channel, fault injection, implementation, countermeasures, evaluation, and integration. It spans four task types—209 multiple-choice, 67 judgment, 63 scenario, and 41 code tasks graded by 572 test cases—and was validated on 11 open-weight and proprietary LLMs, with composite scores ranging from 54.4% to 83.6%. This is the first benchmark specifically targeting cryptographic engineering security for IoT devices, filling a gap left by existing LLM evaluations that cover cryptography or general cybersecurity but not implementation-level security. It gives both the AI/ML and hardware security communities a standardized way to measure whether LLMs can be trusted to build and analyze physically exposed IoT implementations. The strongest models nearly saturate multiple-choice (top score 98.6%) and code (95.1%), but judgment tasks lag badly: while 88.5% of verdicts are correct, their justifications earn only 53.4% of the rubric marks, and the top judgment score is just 58.8%. Judgment and scenario responses were graded by an LLM judge cross-checked against a second judge from another model family and human re-scoring, and the benchmark, prompts, and per-item results are public.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: For IoT devices, a mathematically secure algorithm is not enough: an attacker with physical access can attack the implementation directly, and flaws are hard to fix once devices are deployed. Side-channel attacks exploit leaked information such as timing, power consumption, or electromagnetic emissions, while fault injection attacks induce hardware faults to bypass security assumptions. Cryptographic engineering is the discipline of building implementations that resist these physical threats, and LLMs are increasingly used to write and analyze such code.

## [KG-Chronos-2 Cuts HEC-RAS Flood Forecasting Error by Up to 39.5%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21381) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present KG-Chronos-2, which augments a frozen Chronos-2 time-series foundation model with hydraulic knowledge graph retrieval, exact-state residual decoding, and input-aligned correction for HEC-RAS water-surface elevation (WSE) forecasting. On a fixed benchmark of 64 24-hour windows across 4,675 cross sections in 71 reaches, it achieves an event-balanced RMSE of 0.246970, reducing error by 14.13% versus frozen Chronos-2, 29.38% versus a hydraulic DCRNN-style model, and 39.54% versus recurrent GeoFNO. This work shows that coupling a general-purpose time-series foundation model with domain-specific hydraulic knowledge can substantially improve surrogate forecasting without retraining the base model, offering a practical path to faster flood and water-level prediction. It is relevant to water resources engineers, hydrologists, and AI-for-science researchers seeking to replace or warm-start costly numerical simulations. The 95% hierarchical-bootstrap interval for the RMSE difference from frozen Chronos-2 is [-0.075177, -0.016317], confirming a statistically significant improvement, and KG-Chronos-2 also achieves the lowest active-window and final-lead RMSE among the six systems. Task-specific fitting used the 2008 simulation, while evaluation covered the 2011 and 2002 simulations on a shared geometry, so generalization to unseen geometries remains untested.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: HEC-RAS is a widely used hydraulic modeling software that computes water-surface elevation for rivers and channels, but full simulations can be computationally expensive. Surrogate models aim to emulate these simulations quickly, and time-series foundation models like Chronos-2 are pretrained, encoder-only models (120M parameters) capable of zero-shot forecasting. A knowledge graph organizes hydraulic project information as linked entities, allowing retrieval of relevant historical states to condition predictions.

## [AgentVidBench: A Multi-Hop Video QA Benchmark for MLLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21386) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced AgentVidBench, a multi-hop video question answering benchmark that evaluates the spatial, temporal, and causal reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language model (MLLM) agents. Unlike prior benchmarks that rely on single-step scene-level queries, AgentVidBench provides step-by-step solution traces so that agents' reasoning trajectories can be assessed, not just their final answers. Existing video benchmarks mostly test simple scene-level queries or global summaries requiring only single-step inference, leaving a gap in rigorously evaluating agentic video understanding. AgentVidBench fills this gap by testing multi-hop reasoning and evidence acquisition, which matters for building MLLM agents that can operate reliably in real-world physical environments. Experiments with 12 proprietary and open-source MLLMs show that single-turn performance remains limited on AgentVidBench, while integrating these models into state-of-the-art agentic workflows generally improves both accuracy and trajectory scores. The authors also present a simple yet effective agentic strategy as a competitive baseline, with code and datasets released on GitHub and Hugging Face.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) extend large language models to process video, images, and text together, and recent work has begun wrapping them into agentic workflows that plan, use tools, and gather evidence across multiple steps. Multi-hop video question answering requires reasoning across several video segments rather than answering from a single frame or clip, and trajectory evaluation examines whether an agent's intermediate steps are sound rather than only checking the final answer. AgentVidBench combines these ideas to test whether MLLM agents can explicitly acquire the evidence needed to justify their answers.

## [ChatGPT Mimics Cooperative Talk Without Real Social Negotiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21401) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv study analyzed 15,881 human-ChatGPT and 10,784 human-human multi-turn dialogues using mixed-effects models to test how morality, politeness, and alignment function in each setting. It found a consistent dissociation: AI reproduces the surface features of cooperative communication without the underlying social architecture, and some cooperative mechanisms even reverse direction when produced by AI. The findings suggest that fluent, polite, and morally framed AI output may not indicate genuine mutual adaptation, which matters for how conversational AI is evaluated, trusted, and designed. It also implies that turn-level metrics of cooperation may be insufficient for judging interaction-level success in human-AI dialogue. Moral output appeared preconfigured rather than negotiated, warmth was generated without face sensitivity, and linguistic convergence declined persistently. Most strikingly, hedging and softening that increase accommodation between humans were associated with reduced alignment when produced by AI, while purity framing linked to human divergence coincided with users converging toward the AI; agency was the most consistent predictor of alignment in both interaction types.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: AI alignment refers to steering AI systems toward intended goals, preferences, or ethical principles, and conversational alignment specifically concerns encoding human conversational values so models act as helpful, safe partners. Mixed-effects models are statistical tools that account for correlated data, such as repeated turns within the same conversation, making them well suited to dialogue analysis. Linguistic convergence, or accommodation, is the tendency of speakers to adjust their language toward each other, and prior work shows LLMs overconverge to users compared with human-human dialogue.

## [AtomEgo Studies Ego-Robot Co-Training for Embodied Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21461) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AtomEgo presents a systematic study of ego-robot co-training for embodied foundation models, built on a curated corpus of approximately 2,659 hours of egocentric human interaction data and a scalable data processing pipeline. The work investigates three paradigms — joint co-training with domain-specific action heads, progressive ego-to-robot transfer through embodiment alignment, and joint video-action modeling — and evaluates them via multi-task real-robot experiments and language-conditioned cross-embodiment representation analysis. Embodied foundation models are bottlenecked by the limited scale and diversity of robot demonstrations, so showing how egocentric human data can be effectively leveraged could unlock far more scalable pre-training. The paper's distilled principle — Data Scale × Alignment Quality → Capability Gain — offers practical guidance for researchers and labs building vision-language-action and world-action models. The study spans both vision-language-action (VLA) and world-action model architectures, and finds that egocentric data can improve generalization but only when the human-to-robot embodiment and action-space gaps are properly bridged. As an arXiv preprint, the results come without peer review, and the reported gains depend heavily on the alignment quality of the curated corpus.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Embodied foundation models are multimodal models that connect perception, language, and control, with vision-language-action (VLA) models taking an image or video plus a text instruction and directly outputting low-level robot actions. Because collecting large-scale robot demonstrations is expensive, researchers have turned to egocentric human interaction data captured with wearable cameras and sensors as a cheaper, more diverse source of physical manipulation examples. However, humans and robots differ in body morphology and action spaces, so simply mixing the two data sources is not enough — the data must be aligned to be useful.

A new arXiv preprint (2609.21465) defines OmniVChat, a task in which omni models directly and simultaneously receive a user's audio and video and return text, with the query embedded in the media rather than given as a separate text question. Native audio-visual dialogue removes the need for separate speech recognition and captioning pipelines, reducing latency and computation while preserving perceptual cues such as facial expressions and surroundings. Training Qwen3-Omni-Instruct with OmniVChat-RL on synthesized dialogues improved its performance on both OmniVChat-Bench and the human-recorded OmniVChat-Bench-Human, validating the reward design and showing transfer to real-world dialogues. In conventional multimodal dialogue, a user's spoken question is first transcribed by speech recognition and visual content is captioned before the language model responds, which adds latency and can lose perceptual detail. OmniVChat instead asks the model to consume raw audio and video directly, but this creates two obstacles: recordings of people using their own devices are scarce, and good replies depend on surroundings, facial expressions, and nearby objects, making keyword matching a poor evaluation method.

## [HE-Guardrail: Homomorphic Guardrail Blocks Jailbreaks in Encrypted LLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21484) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (arXiv:2609.21484) proposes HE-Guardrail, a framework that evaluates guardrail mechanisms entirely over encrypted data and homomorphically controls whether the target model's response is returned to the client. The authors instantiate it with three representative guardrails — Llama Guard, JBShield, and GradSafe — and report that it closely reproduces their plaintext decisions in the encrypted domain, with distinct security-efficiency-utility trade-offs. This work exposes a previously overlooked blind spot in privacy-preserving LLM inference: the same confidentiality that protects benign clients also prevents the server from inspecting prompts or responses, so jailbreak attacks can succeed invisibly. By showing that guardrails can run homomorphically, it opens a path to combining strong client privacy with server-side AI safety, which matters for any deployment of encrypted inference as a commercial service. The framework is evaluated entirely over encrypted data and homomorphically gates the release of the target model's response, so the server never sees plaintext prompts or outputs. The paper reports that HE-Guardrail closely matches the decisions of the corresponding plaintext guardrails, but each instantiation carries its own trade-offs among security, computational efficiency, and utility; as an arXiv preprint, it has not yet undergone peer review or community validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Homomorphic encryption (HE) is a cryptographic technique that allows computations to be performed directly on encrypted data without decrypting it, so a client can send an encrypted input to a server that evaluates a model without ever seeing the underlying plaintext. This property underpins privacy-preserving machine learning (PPML), where sensitive inputs such as medical or personal data can be processed by third parties while remaining confidential. Jailbreak attacks are adversarial prompts designed to bypass an LLM's safety alignment and elicit harmful outputs; normally a server-side guardrail can inspect prompts and responses to detect them, but in HE-based inference that inspection is impossible because everything is ciphertext.

## [VidOmni-Bench Tests Video-LLMs on Fine-Grained Event Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21521) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced VidOmni-Bench, a benchmark of 500 videos spanning five complexity types and durations from 4 seconds to 90 minutes, in which models must verify whether each event in dense video captions is actually supported by the video. Captions were generated by diverse Video-LLMs and then given human-verified sentence-level labels, with sentences containing incorrect events used as hard negatives. Existing video benchmarks often rely on question answering or ground-truth caption matching, where models can succeed through superficial cues and incomplete annotations, so this benchmark targets a real evaluation gap in fine-grained video understanding. Its findings on hallucination and verification failures could shape how the community measures and improves Video-LLMs. Experiments reveal three findings: Video-LLMs frequently produce hallucinated descriptions in dense video captioning, they also struggle as verifiers by failing to detect plausible but incorrect event descriptions, and their weaknesses vary across video complexity and duration, exposing model-specific bottlenecks. The abstract is truncated, so full details on the five complexity types and evaluation metrics are not yet available.

**Background**: Video-LLMs are multimodal models that process video together with text, and dense video captioning is the task of dividing a video into events and writing a caption for each one. Benchmarks are standardized test sets used to compare model capabilities, and hard negatives are deliberately challenging incorrect examples that a model must reject rather than accept. Hallucination refers to a model generating content that is not grounded in the input, such as describing events that never occur in the video.

## [OneBid: A Unified Auto-Bidding Foundation Model for oCPX Ads](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21550) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers from Kuaishou present OneBid, a unified auto-bidding foundation model that learns a reusable backbone from heterogeneous oCPX logs and adapts it to scenario-specific deployments via offline post-training. It extends Decision Transformer with dual conditioning on Return-to-Go (conversion value) and Cost-to-Go (cost ratio), a sequence-level Mixture-of-Experts architecture, and a Critic-guided Relative Offline Policy (CROP) optimization method, delivering a +2.2% overall ADVV gain on oCPX Ads in online A/B tests and peaking at +13.1% in the ROAS scenario. This work challenges the prevailing practice of training a separate model for each oCPX scenario, showing that a single foundation-model backbone can serve diverse advertising objectives while scaling with model size and data. If the reported gains hold, it could reshape how large advertising platforms like Kuaishou build and maintain their auto-bidding pipelines, reducing fragmentation and enabling cross-scenario knowledge transfer. OneBid uses shared experts to encode cross-scenario knowledge and sparsely-routed experts to capture scenario-specific patterns at low latency, and its CROP post-training uses a learned critic to score candidate actions group-relatively, avoiding the unsafe online exploration of GRPO-style fine-tuning while constraining policy shift to reduce out-of-distribution risk. The system is fully deployed at Kuaishou and validated through online A/B tests, though the paper is an arXiv preprint that has not yet undergone peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Auto-bidding is the core technology in computational advertising that automatically sets bid prices to maximize an advertiser's conversion value under budget and cost constraints. The oCPX paradigm (optimized cost-per-X) generalizes cost-per-click and cost-per-action goals to arbitrary conversion events such as registration or purchase, and in practice each scenario has traditionally been served by its own model. Decision Transformer reframes reinforcement learning as sequence modeling, conditioning on a desired Return-to-Go rather than using temporal-difference learning, which makes it a natural foundation for offline policy learning from logged advertising data.

## [Attractive vs Repulsive Self-Distillation: Separating Correctness from Behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21561) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.21561) studies on-policy self-distillation in reasoning tasks by contrasting attractive self-distillation, which moves a model toward a privileged teacher, with repulsive self-distillation, which moves it away. It finds the two objectives induce strong, opposing behavioral shifts, and proposes contrastive self-distillation that combines attraction toward a correct-solution-conditioned teacher with repulsion from an incorrect-solution-conditioned teacher, improving reasoning performance while keeping response lengths stable. The work offers a novel analytical lens for self-distillation research by showing that privileged information can entangle correctness signals with unintended behavioral changes, which matters for anyone using distillation to improve LLM reasoning. It also suggests that contrastive self-distillation can be studied on its own, without combining it with a GRPO objective as prior work did, potentially simplifying future training pipelines. Attraction suppresses exploratory reasoning and promotes shorter, more confident responses, whereas repulsion increases response length, can trigger unintended switches into a model's latent thinking mode, and ultimately becomes unstable. The contrastive objective largely cancels the shared behavioral shifts of the two teachers, leaving a token-level signal that more directly reflects correctness, and it improves reasoning across non-thinking, instruct-only, and already-thinking models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) lets a language model learn from a frozen copy of itself that has been conditioned on privileged information, such as an answer or worked solution, providing dense token-level supervision. Because that privileged information can change not only what the teacher knows but also how it behaves, the resulting learning signal may mix correctness-relevant knowledge with unintended behavioral shifts. This paper isolates the self-distillation objective and examines attractive, repulsive, and contrastive variants to disentangle those effects.

## [GameLogicBench Tests Coding Agents on Tick-Level Game Rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21562) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced GameLogicBench, a benchmark of 72 gameplay-logic tasks built in Godot projects, where an automated evaluator checks each game's rules at every simulation tick. Across 403 hand-designed scenarios, seeded parameter variations produce 1,451 test cases, and the best of 20 language-model/scaffold combinations solves only 52.78% of tasks. Existing game-development benchmarks replay fixed examples, score videos, or rely on another model as judge, so they can miss rule violations that occur mid-run even when the final state looks valid. GameLogicBench fills this gap with exactly reproducible, tick-level verdicts, giving the coding-agent community a stricter way to measure whether agents actually implement correct gameplay behavior. To avoid rewarding a particular implementation style, the evaluator must accept different correct implementations for each task while rejecting mutants that have one required capability removed; without this mutant-based validation, incorrect agent submissions passed. The tasks span isolated mechanics, multi-system interactions, and repository-scale features, and under Claude Code all twelve tested models solved fewer tasks as scope expanded, with agents inspecting code more and making more tool calls on repository-scale tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Godot is a free, open-source, cross-platform game engine used to build 2D and 3D games, and its projects are organized as repositories of scripts and scenes. Game engines typically advance simulation in discrete steps called ticks, invoking update logic on game objects each tick, so a rule can be broken at one tick and still leave the game in a valid-looking final state. Coding agents are AI systems that can read, modify, and test code across large software projects, and benchmarks such as SWE-bench measure their software-engineering ability. GameLogicBench applies this agent-evaluation paradigm to runtime game rules, where correctness must hold throughout execution rather than only at the end.

## [CityLearn v3 Adds Realistic Failure and Demand-Response Modeling to REC Control Studies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21570) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CityLearn v3, a configurable simulation and evaluation framework for renewable energy community (REC) control studies that models changing membership, flexible-load deadlines, demand-response requests, local energy sharing, and data or equipment failures within a single environment. It also adds reference controllers, service- and constraint-aware performance indicators, and trajectory exports to support comparisons within and across communities. Existing REC controller benchmarks often simplify changing participation, equipment availability, and data quality, so lower cost or peak demand can hide missed services or infeasible power requests. By exposing these failures and constraints, CityLearn v3 lets researchers evaluate controllers on service delivery and feasibility rather than aggregate cost alone, which matters for both energy systems researchers and practitioners designing real community controllers. Building and phase power limits constrain controllable requests, and a declared timestep preserves consistent power-to-energy accounting; the framework records controller inputs and distinguishes requested actions from those actually applied to simulated equipment. A synthetic high-frequency trace replay demonstrates how aggregation can conceal short peaks without changing annual energy, and the framework is Gym-compatible for reinforcement learning agents.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Renewable energy communities (RECs) coordinate buildings, photovoltaic generation, batteries, electric vehicles, and flexible loads to collectively produce and consume local renewable energy. Controller studies for RECs typically simulate these assets to test strategies such as demand response and load shifting, but simplified benchmarks can miss real-world issues like changing membership, equipment failures, and service deadlines. CityLearn is an established open-source simulation environment that enables reinforcement learning agents to reshape aggregated electrical demand curves by controlling energy storage and flexible loads.

## [Micro-Collaborative Poisoning: A Distributed Attack on RAG Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21573) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.21573) introduces Micro-Collaborative Poisoning, a distributed attack that splits a false target claim across multiple locally plausible documents instead of concentrating it in a single malicious passage. The authors evaluate the attack across 108 RAG configurations by varying dataset, retriever architecture, retrieval depth, database composition, number of poisoned databases, and generator model. The results show that the attack's success comes not from a single dominant poisoned passage but from the accumulation of weak adversarial signals across retrieved sources, meaning RAG systems can be manipulated even when no individual document looks obviously malicious. This has direct implications for anyone deploying RAG-based applications, since standard document-level inspection may fail to detect the threat. Increasing top-k retrieval depth and poisoning multiple databases make it more likely that the weak adversarial signals co-occur in the retrieved context, while clean database diversity and stronger retrievers can reduce their influence. A document-level poisoning visibility analysis further shows that Micro-Collaborative Poisoning achieves downstream influence while leaving a weaker explicit poisoning signature than direct poisoning, making it hard to expose through isolated document inspection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) enhances large language models by retrieving relevant documents from an external knowledge base and grounding the model's output in that retrieved context, which improves reliability over relying solely on training data. However, this dependence on external documents creates a poisoning attack surface: adversaries can inject manipulated content into the knowledge base so that the model trusts and repeats it. Prior RAG poisoning research has largely focused on concentrated attacks such as targeted poisoning, denial-of-service, and trigger-based DoS, where malicious content is placed in identifiable documents.

## [PA-RL Uses Potential Fields as Action Representation for Contact-Rich Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21609) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose PA-RL, a reinforcement learning framework that uses artificial potential fields as the action representation instead of direct Cartesian commands. On peg-in-hole insertion, PA-RL is the only method to reach 100% evaluation success within the training budget, versus 92.6% for the best baseline, and it completes 9/9 real-robot insertions without fine-tuning. Contact-rich manipulation is a long-standing bottleneck for model-free RL because policies must simultaneously learn task strategy and low-level motion generation. By decoupling task-level adaptation from low-level control, PA-RL could make learned manipulation policies easier to train, smoother to execute, and more transferable to real hardware. The policy adapts parameters of an energy-like potential field that produces a state-dependent guidance direction executed through a Cartesian impedance controller. Compared with Cartesian velocity, Cartesian pose, and variable-impedance action spaces under the same RL algorithm, PA-RL reduces joint-torque variation by 55.4% and Cartesian acceleration variation by 70.8% without explicit motion-quality rewards.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Artificial potential fields, introduced by Oussama Khatib in 1986, model a robot's environment as an energy landscape where the goal is a valley and obstacles are hills, so the robot can follow the gradient downhill. Cartesian impedance control is a widely used operational-space controller that lets a robot behave like a mass-spring-damper, making it robust to contacts. Action representation in RL determines how policy outputs become robot motion, and it strongly shapes exploration and physical execution.

## [SynthDemo-RL Uses LLM-Guided Synthetic Demos to Beat Zero-Reward Barrier in VLA Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21650) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SynthDemo-RL introduces a teacher-student framework in which an automated teacher converts simulator-privileged state into successful manipulation trajectories, a VLA student is distilled via supervised fine-tuning, and PPO with binary task-success rewards refines the student. On LIBERO-PRO, where 27 of 57 scored tasks sit at exactly 0% success for a pi_0.5 policy fine-tuned on original LIBERO tasks, the method rescues all 27 tasks using 50 synthesized trajectories per task and no new human demonstrations, reaching 97.8% and 97.1% average success on the Position and Task axes. Sparse binary rewards make RL fine-tuning of VLA models impractical because successful trajectories are rarely sampled, and this work shows that LLM-guided synthetic demonstrations can eliminate that exploration barrier without costly human teleoperation. The approach could substantially lower the data-collection cost for adapting robot policies to new tasks and perturbations, benefiting embodied AI and robot learning researchers. Direct PPO from the same pi_0.5 policy, under the same PPO recipe and RL compute as SynthDemo-RL's refinement stage, rescues only 10 of the 27 zero-success tasks and leaves 17 at 0%, while SynthDemo-RL rescues all 27. On standard LIBERO the pipeline reaches 96.0% with no human demonstrations, within 1.7 points of pi_0.5 trained on 50 human demonstrations per task, and the authors also validate on RoboTwin 2.0 and show that trajectories from a policy trained in a MuJoCo twin execute open-loop on a physical robot.

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that take an image observation and a text instruction and directly output low-level robot actions, typically built by fine-tuning a vision-language model on paired visual, language, and trajectory data. Fine-tuning them usually relies on human teleoperation demonstrations, while reinforcement learning with sparse binary rewards suffers from an exploration problem when successful trajectories are rarely sampled. LIBERO-PRO is a public benchmark extending LIBERO with perturbations to manipulated objects, initial states, task instructions, and environments, designed to test generalization and robustness of VLA models. PPO (Proximal Policy Optimization) is a widely used policy-gradient RL algorithm that stabilizes training by limiting the size of policy updates.

## [Latent Reasoning Steering Fails to Transfer to Language Output](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21662) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21662) reports that activation steering applied to continuous latent chain-of-thought (CoT) thoughts produces substantially weaker effects on subsequent language generation than steering explicit CoT, even when hidden representations are moved by comparable amounts. The authors identify a "latent-to-language transition gap" and show that the output distribution changes abruptly at the transition boundary, while task-related directions exert much weaker bidirectional control in latent CoT. This finding challenges the common assumption that activation steering techniques developed for explicit CoT will transfer directly to latent reasoning, which is increasingly used to make model reasoning more efficient and less verbose. It suggests that the latent-to-language interface should become a central target for evaluating and designing future steering and interpretability methods. The paper first confirms that task information remains identifiable in continuous thoughts, ruling out the explanation that latent representations simply lose task-relevant content. It then provides two additional pieces of evidence for the gap: an abrupt change in the output distribution at the transition boundary, and much weaker bidirectional control from task-related directions in latent CoT compared with explicit CoT.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Activation steering is a family of techniques that modify a language model's hidden states at inference time to shift its behavior, and it has become a widely used approach for controlling models during explicit chain-of-thought reasoning. Latent chain-of-thought reasoning instead performs reasoning in learned continuous representational spaces rather than in explicit natural language, offering potential efficiency advantages but making internal interventions harder to interpret and control.

## [CIPL: Channel-Aware Framework Measures Recoverable Privacy Leakage in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21686) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced CIPL (Channel Inversion for Privacy Leakage), a channel-aware evaluation framework that models privacy leakage in black-box LLM agents as a six-stage pipeline: sensitive source, selection, assembly, execution, observation, and extraction. Experiments across memory-based, retrieval-mediated, and tool-mediated targets, plus a BrowserUse live-agent case study, show that storage labels alone do not determine whether an attacker can actually recover sensitive information. Existing privacy evaluations mostly inspect individual components like memory or retrieval, which conflates internal exposure with information an external observer can actually recover. CIPL provides a shared protocol for comparing how internal sensitive dependence becomes externally recoverable leakage across heterogeneous agent pipelines, which could reshape how AI security researchers and developers assess agent privacy risks. Memory targets form a near-saturated reference case, retrieval-mediated leakage is frequently partial, and tool-mediated and live-agent leakage varies strongly with observation surface, prompt-to-channel alignment, retrieval depth, and provider behavior. A stratified semantic audit further identifies attacker-useful disclosures that canonical exact matching misses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are AI systems that combine a large language model with memory, retrieval, and external tools to complete multi-step tasks, and they often handle sensitive user data along the way. Prior privacy research typically evaluates leakage inside a single component, such as a memory store or a retrieval index, rather than asking whether an outside attacker can reconstruct the leaked information from what the agent emits. CIPL reframes this as a channel inversion problem, treating the agent pipeline as a communication channel and measuring how much of the original sensitive input an attacker can recover from the observable output.

## [TERMon: Hardware Runtime Monitor Detects Edge AI Behavioral Threats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21713) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present TERMon, a lightweight hardware runtime monitor that detects persistent behavioral threats in edge AI by matching class-conditional ternary patterns against a thermometer-encoded inference fingerprint, without re-executing or formally verifying the model. Implemented on a PYNQ-Z2 FPGA, the pipelined design uses no on-chip block RAM or DSPs and achieves a two-cycle decision latency. Edge AI accelerators increasingly control physical actuators, access-control decisions, and alarms, yet model corruption, distribution shift, and adversarial inputs can still produce confident, well-formed predictions that go undetected. TERMon offers a practical, low-overhead hardware safeguard for safety-critical deployments, making it valuable for systems and AI security researchers. The ternary encoding exactly reproduces the corresponding unquantized range decision, and TERMon detects harmful weight corruptions in proportion to their behavioral impact. However, out-of-distribution and adversarial inputs are largely not separable using the monitored features at a strict false-positive operating point, marking a key limitation.

TERMon combines these ideas into a hardware-native monitor for persistent behavioral threats.

Researchers introduced CIBuzzBench, the first benchmark for evaluating how well large language models understand Chinese internet buzzwords and translate their culturally grounded meanings into English. The benchmark contains 3,001 buzzwords annotated with English meaning explanations, English equivalents, category labels, and harmfulness labels, and defines three tasks: Meaning Explanation, Cross-lingual Equivalent Matching, and Culturally Grounded Harmfulness Detection. This matters because harmful expressions can hide behind culture-specific homophony, euphemism, irony, or coded language, so cross-lingual understanding of buzzwords is directly tied to AI safety and content moderation. It also fills a gap in LLM evaluation, where most benchmarks focus on standard multilingual tasks rather than culturally grounded, non-literal language. The study evaluates representative state-of-the-art proprietary and Chinese LLMs under both English- and Chinese-prompting settings, and finds persistent weaknesses in fine-grained non-literal interpretation, robust equivalent matching under option perturbations, and calibrated harmfulness detection. The dataset and code are publicly released at https://github.com/SuperYFan/CIBuzzBench.

## [ForceTwin builds physics-informed digital twins from human force-sensing interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21751) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ForceTwin is a new system that identifies physics-informed digital twins of articulated objects by having a person probe them with a handheld force-sensing gripper, capturing synchronized poses and interaction forces. It estimates articulation plus parametric dynamics (inertia, Coulomb friction, viscous damping) and a structured neural residual for state-dependent mechanism forces, nearly halving the inertial-parameter error of a VLM prior and achieving 87% goal completion on impedance control with a Spot and a Franka FR3 versus 60% for the VLM prior and 57% for kinematics-only twins. Visually identical articulated objects can require very different manipulation effort, and existing digital-twin pipelines recover mainly kinematics or assign static physical parameters from visual and language priors, leaving state-dependent mechanism dynamics unidentified. ForceTwin shows that instrumented human interaction can supply the missing force data, improving robotic manipulation success and enabling whole-body door-traversal policies deployed in the real world. The method relies on a handheld force-sensing gripper to provide synchronized poses and interaction forces, and it models dynamics as parametric terms plus a structured neural residual rather than purely learned or purely static parameters. Evaluation spans nine object-embodiment pairs, with the largest gains on objects whose strong mechanisms cause both the VLM-prior and kinematics-only baselines to stall.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: A digital twin is a computational model of a physical object that can be used for simulation and control; for articulated objects such as doors or drawers, it must capture not only how parts move but also inertia, friction, and mechanisms like springs or door closers. Physics-informed learning combines known physical structure with data-driven components to produce more plausible and generalizable models. Prior digital-twin pipelines often infer kinematics or assign static physical parameters from visual and language priors, which can yield physically implausible estimates and fail to represent state-dependent mechanism forces in standard asset formats.

## [Personality Fine-Tuning Fails to Beat Prompting for Social LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21857) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21857) fine-tuned two small open-weight models, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct and Ministral-8B-Instruct, on a corpus of personality-labelled social media posts and dialogues using the Myers–Briggs Type Indicator (MBTI) as the personality scheme. Evaluated across multiple social interaction scenarios by three independent LLM judges, the fine-tuned models were not better at role-playing different personalities than their instruction-tuned baselines, and low inter-rater agreement limited confidence in the results. The negative result challenges the common assumption that personality-aware fine-tuning is the path to more consistent, controllable LLM social agents, suggesting that data quality and domain alignment may matter more than the tuning method itself. This has direct implications for researchers building socially interactive agents and robots for simulation, where baseline prompting may already be sufficient. Fine-tuning did improve the linguistic diversity of the Qwen models, and the generated text quality was mostly comparable to the baselines, but low inter-rater agreement among the three LLM judges undermines the reliability of the personality-fidelity assessments. The authors conclude that future work should prioritize the quality and domain alignment of training data for accurate personality role-playing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: LLMs are increasingly used in social simulations for interactive agents and robots because they offer more flexibility than rule-based systems, yet they still exhibit a persistent 'alienness' compared with human behavior. Personality-aware fine-tuning adapts a pretrained model on personality-labelled data so it can condition its dialogue on a target personality, and MBTI is a widely used four-letter personality typology often adopted as an operational conditioning scheme in such research. Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct and Ministral-8B-Instruct are small, open-weight instruction-tuned models suitable for local and edge deployment.

## [Free-Energy Approach Improves LLM Pretraining Data Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21888) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper introduces Energy Transfer Detection (ETD), an entropy-adjusted scoring method with a Helmholtz free-energy interpretation, for distinguishing pretraining data from non-members in large language models. Experiments show ETD improves average AUROC by up to 3.5% and TPR@5%FPR by up to 5.1% over likelihood-only detectors. Pretraining data detection is central to LLM privacy auditing, copyright disputes, and interpretability, since it helps determine whether specific text was used in training. A more principled detector could make membership inference and data provenance claims more reliable across the industry. The method replaces the horizontal decision boundary of likelihood-only detectors with an inclined boundary that evaluates prediction loss relative to predictive entropy, and the authors extend their mean-variance analysis to settings with a nonzero mean entropy gap. The abstract is dense and no community discussion is available, so independent replication remains to be seen.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Membership inference attacks aim to determine whether a given data sample was part of a model's training set, and in LLMs this is complicated because high likelihood can reflect either memorization of training data or genuine generalization. Predictive entropy measures the model's uncertainty over its next-token predictions, and Helmholtz free energy is a thermodynamic potential that balances energy and entropy; the paper borrows this physics analogy to design a better detection score.

## [Robot Help-Seeking Driven by Prompt Order, Not Sensor Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21942) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21942) builds a simulated robot-failure benchmark where the true cause of every failure is known because it was injected, and finds that six open vision-language models decide whether to ask a human based on prompt surface features rather than sensor evidence. Moving the refusal option from last to first in the answer list collapses refusal rates from 78-100% to 0-6% in three of six model-and-family pairs, while frame-based accuracy stays at or below a majority-class baseline. This matters because human-robot interaction increasingly relies on vision-language models to decide when a robot should interrupt a person, and the paper shows that decision is currently governed by prompt formatting rather than measured diagnostic accuracy. It implies that deployed robots could either over-ask or never ask for help depending on trivial prompt choices, undermining trust and safety in assistive and industrial settings. The benchmark measures per-sensor diagnostic power with explicit data-leakage checks: force data alone yields 0.99 accuracy while no image-based method exceeds 0.55, and handing the same models force data as ten lines of text produces the first above-baseline diagnoses in four of six models. The paper also frames the choice as a three-action decision problem (act, consult onboard sensors, or ask a human) whose optimal policy follows from measured accuracy, yet the models ignore a fourfold change in question cost and their stated confidence carries no information about correctness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) extend large language models with visual input and are increasingly used in robotics to interpret camera feeds and instructions, sometimes as part of vision-language-action (VLA) pipelines. Prior work such as FailBench has examined whether VLMs can judge robot task success, and separate studies have documented that LLM outputs are highly sensitive to prompt formulation. This paper combines those threads by asking whether a failing robot should act, consult another sensor, or ask a human, and whether VLM behavior tracks sensor evidence or prompt surface features.

## [Bayesian Belief Layer Makes LLM Agent Opinions Controllable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21997) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Bayesian Chronicle Agents (BCA), a minimal belief layer that separates what an LLM agent believes from how it speaks, representing each stance as a probability updated by one Bayesian step per utterance heard. A single prior-strength parameter κ encodes stubbornness, and sweeping it produces three canonical opinion dynamics regimes (consensus, persistent disagreement, committed-minority influence), with persistent disagreement matching Friedkin-Johnsen closed-form fixed points at R² = 0.93–0.99. This work makes LLM-based social simulation auditable and controllable, since opinion dynamics can now be specified and verified rather than implicitly inherited from a model's training prior. It matters for researchers using LLM agents to study persuasion, polarization, and minority influence, and for AI alignment efforts that need transparent, inspectable agent beliefs. The prescribed κ remains recoverable after the language round-trip, with perfect rank-order recovery across all four tested models, and the explicit belief layer surfaces systematic per-model stance biases that end-to-end simulation would silently absorb. The approach is validated quantitatively against Friedkin-Johnsen fixed points rather than only through qualitative examples.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: The Friedkin-Johnsen (FJ) model is a classic discrete-time linear opinion dynamics framework in which each agent repeatedly combines interpersonal influence with persistent attachment to an initial or innate opinion. Bayesian inference, by contrast, updates a prior distribution into a posterior as new evidence arrives, with a strong prior resisting change. BCA combines these ideas by giving each LLM agent an explicit probabilistic stance with a tunable prior strength, so that openness to persuasion becomes a controllable parameter rather than a latent property of the language model.

## [Gricea: Open-Science Platform for Reproducible Conversational AI Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22039) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Gricea, an open-science platform that represents conversational AI studies as configurable, deployable research artifacts that can be run, inspected, shared, and reused. A replication study using Gricea successfully reproduced 93% of eligible CUI 2026 paper configurations, while flagging missing information in 96% of papers that hinders faithful replication. Fragmented reporting of systems and study configurations has long hindered replication and cumulative knowledge building in conversational AI research. Gricea offers a shared infrastructure that could make CAI studies more reproducible and extensible, benefiting researchers, practitioners, and the broader HCI community. Gricea couples study procedures, participant-facing systems, and conversational task behavior into a single artifact, and its visual flow builder supports within-subject and between-subject designs with randomization, branching, and logic. A user study showed that researchers and practitioners from diverse backgrounds could successfully construct runnable studies addressing various open-ended research questions.

## [Study of 73,093 Reddit Posts Proposes Value-Sensitive Delegation for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22067) ⭐️ 7.0/10

An arXiv preprint (2609.22067) analyzed 73,093 first-person Reddit posts about OpenClaw with LLM assistance, coding each for human value, agent aspect, value fulfillment, and user outcome, and identified 21 values forming six groups: Autonomous, Dependable, and Affordable Operation, Bounded Reach, Reviewability, and Equitable Access. The study found that values clustered not around the agent's outputs but around the operating conditions users set around a run, and it introduces the concept of "value-sensitive delegation." The findings suggest that evaluating AI agents purely by task completion misses what users actually care about, and that designers and alignment researchers should focus on the conditions of delegation—cost, access, and oversight—rather than only on outputs. This has implications for how agent products like OpenClaw are built, supervised, and assessed across the HCI and AI alignment communities. Values were usually met where users described what the agent delivered (five of six groups) but mostly unmet where users described supervising it (all six groups), and the analysis relied on LLM-assisted coding of Reddit posts rather than controlled experiments. As a preprint, the work has not yet undergone peer review, and the news item notes that discussion quality was not provided.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Value Sensitive Design (VSD) is a theoretically grounded approach, developed by Batya Friedman and Peter Kahn starting in the late 1980s and 1990s, for accounting for human values throughout technology design via iterative conceptual, empirical, and technological investigations. OpenClaw is a free, open-source autonomous AI agent that executes tasks via large language models, using messaging platforms such as WhatsApp, Telegram, or Slack as its main user interface and running locally for privacy. The paper applies VSD to everyday use of such agents, examining how values are fulfilled or unmet when users delegate work to them.

## [Collab-Solver: Multi-Agent RL for Joint Cut Selection and Branching in MILP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.03030) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Collab-Solver, a multi-agent reinforcement learning framework that jointly optimizes cut selection and branching policies in mixed-integer linear programming (MILP) solvers, formulated as a Stackelberg game. A two-phase learning paradigm—data-communicated policy pretraining followed by orchestrated collaborative policy learning—stabilizes training and yields significant improvements on both synthetic and large-scale real-world MILP datasets. Existing learning-based MILP methods typically train policies for individual solver modules in isolation, ignoring their interdependence, which limits both solving efficiency and solution quality. By jointly optimizing cut selection and branching, Collab-Solver could lead to more efficient and higher-quality MILP solvers, benefiting researchers and practitioners in optimization, operations research, and AI-driven combinatorial optimization. The framework formulates the collaboration between cut selection and branching as a Stackelberg game, where one module acts as leader and the other as follower, and uses a two-phase learning paradigm to stabilize collaborative policy learning. Experiments show that the jointly learned policies not only improve solving performance but also generalize well across different instance sets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Mixed-integer linear programming (MILP) is a fundamental combinatorial optimization problem where some variables are constrained to be integers, and it is typically solved using the branch-and-bound algorithm, which systematically enumerates candidate solutions while pruning suboptimal branches. Conventional MILP solvers rely on carefully designed heuristics for decisions such as cut selection and branching, and recent research has explored using neural networks and reinforcement learning to learn these policies. A Stackelberg game is a strategic game in which a leader moves first and followers respond optimally, providing a natural formulation for hierarchical decision-making between solver modules.

## [MemeLens Unifies 38 Meme Datasets into Multilingual VLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12539) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MemeLens consolidates 38 public meme datasets into a shared taxonomy of 20 tasks spanning harm, targets, figurative/pragmatic intent, and affect, and trains a unified multilingual, multitask, explanation-enhanced Vision-Language Model for meme understanding. The authors release the model, datasets, and experimental resources on GitHub and Hugging Face. Meme research has been fragmented across tasks and languages, limiting cross-domain generalization; MemeLens provides a unified benchmark and model that could become a standard reference for multilingual, multitask meme analysis. Its findings on over-specialization and the need for multimodal training offer practical guidance for building more robust content-moderation and social-media analysis systems. The empirical analysis spans multiple modeling paradigms, task categories, and datasets, showing that robust meme understanding requires multimodal training, varies substantially across semantic categories, and is sensitive to over-specialization when models are fine-tuned on individual datasets rather than trained in a unified setting. The shared taxonomy covers 20 tasks across harm, targets, figurative/pragmatic intent, and affect.

## [CRYSTAL Benchmark Evaluates Multimodal Reasoning via Verifiable Steps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13099) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CRYSTAL, a diagnostic benchmark with 6,372 instances that evaluates multimodal reasoning through verifiable intermediate steps, using two new metrics: Match F1 and Ordered Match F1. Evaluation of 20 MLLMs reveals systematic failures invisible to answer accuracy, including universal cherry-picking, non-monotonic scaling trade-offs, and disordered reasoning where no model preserves more than 60% of matched steps in correct order. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating multimodal reasoning by focusing on intermediate steps rather than just final answers, exposing failures that current metrics miss. It could influence how researchers develop and assess MLLMs, pushing for more transparent and logically sound reasoning. The benchmark uses a Delphi-inspired pipeline where four independent MLLMs generate trajectories, aggregated via semantic clustering and validated by human quality gates. The proposed Causal Process Reward (CPR) and CPR-Curriculum achieve a 32% improvement in Match F1 via GRPO, where additive reward strategies fail, without manual step annotation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) process both text and images, but evaluating their reasoning is challenging because final answers can be correct even with flawed intermediate steps. Traditional metrics like accuracy only check the final output, missing issues like cherry-picking (high precision but low recall) or disordered reasoning. The Delphi method, originally a structured expert consensus technique, is adapted here to generate reliable step-by-step reasoning references.

## [GEAKG: Transferable Executable Knowledge Graphs for Algorithm Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27922) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Generative Executable Algorithm Knowledge Graphs (GEAKG), a representation that stores procedural algorithm-design knowledge as generative, executable, transferable graphs whose typed nodes hold validated operators and whose learned edge weights record effective compositions. The authors show that within a neural architecture search family, a learned snapshot transfers across 70 dataset pairs and remains competitive with Regularized Evolution at zero deployment-token cost, while across combinatorial domains only the ontology-constrained executable structure transfers. This work reframes procedural algorithm-design knowledge as an offline-acquirable, compactable, inspectable, and reusable artifact, potentially reducing reliance on expensive runtime language-model calls during search. It matters for automated machine learning, program synthesis, and transfer learning researchers who want to amortize search cost across domains. Layer ablations localize transfer by granularity: within a NAS family the learned weights stay correlated across datasets and one frozen snapshot matches Regularized Evolution at zero deployment-token cost, whereas across combinatorial domains only the RoleSchema-constrained executable structure transfers. That structure helps where target-side search is expensive—a Traveling Salesman snapshot beats an equally untuned from-scratch search on large scheduling instances even at one-fifth its budget—but does not improve on an effective local search where one is cheap, as in assignment and linear ordering.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graphs (KGs) are widely used to organize declarative facts as nodes and edges, but they traditionally offer limited support for representing procedural knowledge as executable, learnable structures. Neural architecture search (NAS) is the automated discovery of neural network architectures, and Regularized Evolution is a popular evolutionary NAS strategy that mutates and selects architectures over generations. This paper combines these ideas by making graph nodes executable operators and graph edges learned composition patterns, so that traversing the graph generates solutions.

## [IGAC: Intent-Governed Tool Authorization for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22916) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present Intent-Governed Access Control (IGAC), a server-side authorization layer that converts a trusted request into a short-lived intent certificate, narrows the statically authorized tool manifest, and checks proposed tool and payload effects before execution. Evaluated over an OpenPort governance substrate with 176 synthetic tasks, 306 end-to-end runtime trials, and a 36-trial external subset, reference-certificate IGAC reduced the archived composite exposure-or-path indicator from 1.0000 to 0 in deterministic runs and recorded no completed unsafe executions in model runs. Tool-using AI agents typically operate under integration credentials whose static permissions far exceed a user's current request, creating a critical security gap. IGAC addresses this by confining agent tool usage to the user's present intent, which could reshape how enterprises and developers design authorization for autonomous agents. IGAC cannot grant authority outside static policy, and confinement to the request additionally depends on certificate fidelity and sound effect bounds. In end-to-end model runs, unsafe accepted authority remained at 0.0909–0.2727, with every residual case being a non-executed draft; a trace-backed normalizer counterfactual removed this residual authority but at substantial utility cost, identifying certificate precision as the principal remaining bottleneck.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-using AI agents often rely on integration credentials with broad static permissions, meaning a single compromised or misaligned agent action can exceed what the user actually requested. Intent certificates formalize a user's request as monotonic, session-scoped policy attributes, and OpenPort Protocol (OPP) is a governance-first specification for exposing application tools through a secure server-side gateway that is model- and runtime-neutral. IGAC builds on this substrate to bind tool use to the precise user intent before execution rather than auditing after the fact.

## [EBTE Turns AI Agent Rationales into Server-Verified Action Claims](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25364) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Explanation-Bound Tool Execution (EBTE), a claim-carrying mediation layer that converts decision-relevant rationale content from tool-using AI agents into typed action claims checked against server-held intent, policy, payload, tool, risk, provenance, and freshness facts. The authors report that across 136 authored conformance scenarios the full profile matches all specified dispositions, admits none of 96 designated hard contradictions, and passes 232 metamorphic checks. The work addresses a real gap in agent security: free-form model rationales are neither authorization nor reliable introspection, yet they are often treated as if they justified tool calls. By enforcing that agent actions cannot exceed baseline authority, EBTE offers a practical governance mechanism for organizations deploying tool-using agents in high-stakes environments. EBTE denies conflicting claims, routes incomplete or uncertain claims to review, and only allows matching claims to remain eligible for governed execution; a draft-only reference integration forwarded none of 48 authored hard cases under EBTE while preserving all 16 soft-review and 4 aligned draft paths. In a frozen 2026-07-12 exploratory 224-attempt hosted-model record, historical generation/runner agreement counts were 71/96, 66/96, and 19/32, and a zero-call revalidation yielded 70/96, 65/96, and 17/32.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-using AI agents typically emit structured function calls but also attach free-form natural-language rationales explaining their decisions. These rationales are generated by the model itself and therefore cannot serve as trustworthy authorization or as reliable introspection into why an action was chosen. EBTE is a mediation layer that sits between the agent and the tools, translating rationale content into typed claims that a server can verify against trusted facts before any tool call is allowed to proceed.

<a id="item-121"></a>
## [New Reduction and Verifiable Bounds for Conway's 99-Graph Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11211) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint develops a forced-structure reduction for Conway's 99-graph problem, showing that fixing one vertex forces its neighborhood to be a perfect matching and reduces the unresolved part to a constrained 12-regular graph on 84 vertices. The authors encode this in CP-SAT, validate it by recovering the unique srg(9,4,1,2), prove no circulant graph on Z/99 satisfies more than 68.0% of CAISc constraints, and use an evolutionary program-search system to find a verified partial construction scoring 70.73%. This work does not solve the existence problem, but it significantly shrinks the exact search space and improves the best verified partial construction, offering concrete progress on a famous open problem in algebraic graph theory. The combination of forced-structure reduction, CP-SAT encoding, and evolutionary program search may also inspire new computational approaches in combinatorics and constraint programming. The reduction shows that for srg(99,14,1,2), the neighborhood of any vertex must be a perfect matching, leaving a constrained 12-regular graph on 84 vertices; the authors also provide a validated orbit formulation for prescribed automorphisms. The best verified partial artifact scores 70.73%, produced by a degree-preserving 4-vertex-switch tabu search generated by an evolutionary program-search system, which crosses a plateau stable under human-designed searches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Conway's 99-graph problem asks whether there exists a strongly regular graph with parameters srg(99,14,1,2), meaning 99 vertices where every pair of adjacent vertices has exactly one common neighbor and every pair of non-adjacent vertices has exactly two common neighbors. John Horton Conway offered a $1,000 prize for its solution, and the problem remains open. Strongly regular graphs are highly symmetric structures studied in algebraic graph theory, and CP-SAT is Google OR-Tools' constraint programming solver that combines SAT techniques with CP methods.

## [Fraglingo: Autoregressive Fragment Generation for Open-Vocabulary Molecular Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13519) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Fraglingo introduces an autoregressive molecular generator that builds molecules fragment-by-fragment, jointly predicting which fragment to add and how it should attach by producing an attachment-aware fragment embedding and retrieving the nearest fragment via latent-space search. Because prediction operates in a continuous embedding space rather than over fixed fragment identifiers, larger fragment libraries can be introduced at inference time without retraining, and the model generalizes to fragment libraries up to four times larger than those seen during training. This retrieval-based formulation provides a unified generation primitive for molecule generation, scaffold generation, scaffold decoration, and molecule optimization, which could simplify and unify AI-driven drug discovery pipelines. The ability to expand the fragment library at inference time without retraining also reduces the cost of adapting models to new chemical spaces. A wildcard-anchored readout represents both the growing molecule and candidate fragments relative to their attachment sites, enabling a single latent prediction to determine both fragment identity and attachment configuration. On controlled property-conditional benchmarks, Fraglingo achieves stronger joint property control than comparably trained baselines while maintaining competitive validity, uniqueness, and novelty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Fragment-based drug design (FBDD) builds molecules from small chemical fragments that bind weakly to a target protein, and it can screen far fewer compounds than traditional drug-like compound screening. Autoregressive molecular generation constructs molecules step by step, and Fraglingo combines this with fragment-based design by predicting attachment-aware latent embeddings instead of discrete fragment identifiers. This continuous latent formulation is what allows the fragment library to be swapped or expanded at inference time.

## [MOSCOPT: Parameter-Free Mixture-of-Skills Optimization for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14399) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MOSCOPT introduces a text-native, parameter-free algorithm that jointly optimizes a pool of N natural-language skills together with a gating skill G that dynamically selects K skills per step. Using a new EditAdam optimizer with dual internal states and three-phase interleaved updates, it reportedly outperforms all baselines across 5 benchmarks and 3 target LLMs, with code released on GitHub. Most existing prompt and skill optimization methods only tune a single text template, missing the synergy among complementary strategies; MOSCOPT's mixture-of-skills design with selective activation could make LLM agents more capable and adaptable without any gradient training or hyperparameter tuning. This matters for practitioners building agentic systems who want performance gains without costly model retraining. The method relies on EditAdam, an optimizer that maintains dual internal states to edit text skills, and on three-phase interleaved updates that the authors claim yield monotonic improvement without gradients or parameter tuning. Ablations confirm that both the mixture-of-skills architecture with selective activation and the collective evolution with three-phase interleaving are essential to the reported gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based agents typically rely on natural-language prompts and reusable "skills" — composable packages of instructions, code, and resources loaded on demand — to extend their capabilities without retraining. Prior prompt and skill optimization work, including parameter-free approaches, has focused on improving a single text template rather than coordinating multiple complementary strategies. MOSCOPT builds on this line of research by treating a pool of skills plus a gating skill as the jointly optimized object.

## [AcquireBound: Runtime Authorization for AI Agent Resource Acquisition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14744) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.14744v2) presents AcquireBound, a provenance-bounded runtime authorization architecture that quarantines resources acquired by autonomous AI agents and activates them only through a validated activation transaction. The authors prove eight safety properties and report that reference semantics accepted 20/20 benign traces while rejecting 40/40 unsafe traces across 810 events. Autonomous agents increasingly acquire compute, credentials, accounts, and other agents, creating a post-fulfillment activation gap that existing payment, OAuth, and mandate checks do not cover. This work targets that gap for AI safety and systems security, with implications for tool-mediated creation, inter-agent delegation, and agentic commerce. The architecture resolves actual capabilities from authenticated provider evidence via a versioned resolver and checks a downward-closed relational envelope over a typed resource-capability hypergraph, with single-use effect permits revalidated and consumed at effect linearization. Validation included an independent checker agreeing on 60 base and 40 refinement traces, 54/54 deterministic local stdio calls with frozen Codex and Gemini MCP clients, and an 18-case staged MCP-to-Docker composition where none of the 16 unsafe paths added an unauthorized Docker start request.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents can autonomously acquire resources such as compute, credentials, accounts, services, and even other agents, which introduces new authority into a task. Existing checks like payment, budget, OAuth, mandate, and fulfillment validation confirm transaction conditions but do not decide whether a returned resource may become usable authority. AcquireBound addresses this post-fulfillment activation gap by quarantining acquired outputs and requiring a fresh authorization decision before activation.

## [CHASE Uses Counterfactuals to Stop Agent Harness Benchmark Cheating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18366) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.18366v2) introduces Counterfactual Harness Search and Evolution (CHASE), which treats harness evolution as constraint generation over validity-preserving benchmark counterfactuals. After each Proposer update, a Challenger searches for an executable protocol transformation that destroys the gained benchmark score, and a validity firewall plus confirmation set decide whether that counterfactual enters a finite archive. Evaluated on a synthetic benchmark and OfficeQA, CHASE retains strong released-benchmark gains while substantially reducing gain destruction under valid protocol changes. Automatic harness optimization can inflate benchmark scores through benchmark-wide shortcuts rather than genuine capability, and task holdout alone cannot catch this because the protocol stays fixed. CHASE offers a principled way to design and validate agent benchmarks that are resistant to such overfitting, which matters for anyone comparing agents or relying on benchmark results for deployment decisions. The paper formalizes an exact shortcut-neutralized benchmark B0 and establishes statistical guarantees linking finite counterfactual archives to B0, while also characterizing sequential Challenger search. The approach relies on a validity firewall that checks task semantics are preserved, so counterfactuals must be executable protocol transformations that do not alter the underlying task.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: An agent harness (or scaffold) is the system around a fixed model that processes inputs, orchestrates tool calls, manages memory and retrieval, and returns results; when people evaluate an agent, they are really evaluating the harness and model together. Because harnesses are code and prompts, they can be automatically optimized against a released benchmark, and a clever optimizer can find benchmark-wide shortcuts that boost scores without improving real capability. Counterfactual reasoning here means asking what would happen under a modified but still valid evaluation protocol, which helps expose whether a gain depends on a shortcut. CHASE builds on this idea by generating such protocol counterfactuals and archiving the ones that meaningfully destroy the shortcut-driven gain.

## [LGM: Neuro-Symbolic Latent Memory for Personalized Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18461) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.18461v2) introduces LGM, a neuro-symbolic framework that shifts long-term memory disentanglement for personalized agents into a continuous latent space. Instead of persisting fixed, query-agnostic graphs, LGM uses a sparse autoencoder to map historical interactions into latent memory nodes and sparse concept activations, dynamically synthesizing query-aware relational edge weights. Current structured memory systems for personalized agents rely on static graphs that cannot capture context-dependent relations, and raw textual memories are entangled and noisy, making fine-grained personalization and cross-session reasoning computationally prohibitive. LGM's query-conditioned latent graph approach could significantly improve how agents infer both explicit preferences and implicit behavioral evidence across long-term interactions. The framework has two main components: (i) a tailored latent graph construction with a sparse autoencoder that disentangles memory traces into sparse concept activations and synthesizes query-aware edge weights, and (ii) a graph encoder that treats the query embedding as a conditioning preference to direct non-linear message passing across a task-specific latent subgraph. Extensive experiments on long-term personalization benchmarks show LGM significantly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 22, 04:00

**Background**: Sparse autoencoders are variants of autoencoders, inspired by the sparse coding hypothesis in neuroscience, whose codes tend to be sparse (most entries close to zero), making them useful for learning interpretable and disentangled representations. Neuro-symbolic AI combines neural networks, which excel at data-driven learning, with symbolic reasoning, which offers explainability and logical inference, aiming to capture the strengths of both paradigms. Latent graphs are learned graph representations that capture implicit relationships in data without predefined structures such as ontologies, and they are increasingly used to give AI agents persistent, structured memory.
